     1	Env-gated CTA cap for the ragged-all-to-all device kernel (H11).
     2	Base: marin-community/xla @ c9526e8c02727f1e1e2c631e2b50c53b8839fb92
     3	(the revision the campaign's pinned jax_cuda13_pjrt-0.11.1+marin.c9526e8c0272
     4	wheel is built from). Default behavior is bit-identical to that wheel; the
     5	knob engages only when XLA_RAGGED_A2A_DK_CTAS_PER_SM is set to 1..7 in the
     6	task environment.
     7	
     8	--- a/xla/backends/gpu/runtime/ragged_all_to_all_thunk.h
     9	+++ b/xla/backends/gpu/runtime/ragged_all_to_all_thunk.h
    10	@@
    11	+#include <cstdlib>
    12	+
    13	@@
    14	   // barrier/signal slots reserved when creating the device communicator (the
    15	   // kernel indexes its cooperative barrier by blockIdx.x, so registration
    16	   // must cover the launched grid). All ranks launch the same grid, which the
    17	   // cross-rank cooperative barriers require: every CTA must be resident
    18	   // simultaneously, so the size is kGridSmMultiplier CTAs of 128 threads per
    19	   // SM. Callers pass the SM count from
    20	   // se::DeviceDescription::core_count(); all participating ranks are expected
    21	   // to be homogeneous so every rank arrives at the same value.
    22	+  //
    23	+  // XLA_RAGGED_A2A_DK_CTAS_PER_SM (1..kGridSmMultiplier, default
    24	+  // kGridSmMultiplier) caps the per-SM CTA count so the kernel's resident
    25	+  // register/thread footprint can be traded against copy bandwidth: the
    26	+  // balanced copy is grid-size-agnostic (per-CTA element ranges and the GIN
    27	+  // put loop both derive from the launched grid), a regular (non-cooperative)
    28	+  // launch keeps a smaller grid trivially resident, and every barrier/signal
    29	+  // registration below routes through this same function, so launch and
    30	+  // registration stay consistent. The value must be identical on all ranks.
    31	   static int32_t DeviceKernelCtaCount(int core_count) {
    32	-    return std::max<int32_t>(kGridSmMultiplier * core_count,
    33	+    static const int32_t multiplier = [] {
    34	+      const char* env = std::getenv("XLA_RAGGED_A2A_DK_CTAS_PER_SM");
    35	+      const int parsed = env == nullptr ? 0 : std::atoi(env);
    36	+      return (parsed >= 1 && parsed <= kGridSmMultiplier)
    37	+                 ? parsed
    38	+                 : kGridSmMultiplier;
    39	+    }();
    40	+    return std::max<int32_t>(multiplier * core_count,
    41	                              kMinDeviceKernelCtaCount);
    42	   }
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Submit one hero EP64 arm for the 8753-mfu loop (research/mcwitt/8753-mfu-loop, #8753 head base).
     3	#
     4	# Protocol (fixed for every iteration so arms stay comparable; see DESIGN.md):
     5	#   - one NVL72 rack on cw-us-east-08a, PRODUCTION priority allowed with job RUNTIME <1h
     6	#     (user 2026-08-28): ARM_TIMEOUT 28800s covers Kueue queue wait plus run (occupancy is
     7	#     intrinsically bounded by the absolute NUM_STEPS stop); watchdog.sh COMPILE_CEILING=1800 +
     8	#     STEP_BUDGET=1200 enforce the runtime cap from admission
     9	#   - restore from the live hero's step-30000 checkpoint, mixture data (trained-router routing);
    10	#     NUM_STEPS is the ABSOLUTE stop step (checkpoint step + window), never a relative count
    11	#   - no checkpoints, no eval; profiler off on SCORED arms (a profile tail on an otherwise-scored
    12	#     draw is allowed: PROFILE_STEPS>0 with PROFILE_START_STEP as an ABSOLUTE step past the
    13	#     scoring window, e.g. 30021)
    14	#   - no MARIN_PJRT_WHEEL overlay: the branch's uv.lock pins jax_cuda13_pjrt 0.11.1+marin.c9526e8c0272
    15	#   - JAX_COMPILATION_CACHE_DIR rotated per run id (clique-init deadlock dodge); a RESUBMITTED
    16	#     arm must use a fresh RID (and VERSION): reusing one merges W&B histories, reuses the
    17	#     leader-populated compile cache (clique-deadlock recipe), and can vacuously reuse artifacts
    18	#
    19	# usage: RID=<run-id> VERSION=<calver> [REPO=<worktree>] [EXTRA_LAUNCH_ARGS="..."] arm.sh
    20	set -euo pipefail
    21	
    22	: "${WANDB_API_KEY:?set WANDB_API_KEY}"
    23	RID="${RID:?set RID}"
    24	VERSION="${VERSION:?set VERSION -- bump it per arm or the artifact layer reuses the last run}"
    25	
    26	# The watchdog addresses jobs at /mwittmann/...; make the submission land there no matter what
    27	# the caller's shell has. (-e below only sets the remote env.)
    28	export IRIS_USER=mwittmann
    29	
    30	LOOP_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
    31	REPO="${REPO:-$(cd "${LOOP_DIR}/../.." && pwd)}"
    32	
    33	# Iris bundles the working tree, not HEAD: uncommitted edits (including another session writing
    34	# to a shared checkout) would ship silently and break arm attribution. Only the loop dir's DATA
    35	# files (logs, tsv, review transcripts) are exempt; uncommitted protocol scripts count as dirty,
    36	# since arms.tsv records HEAD as the arm's provenance.
    37	dirty="$(git -C "$REPO" status --porcelain | grep -Ev "autoresearch/.*\.(log|out|txt|tsv)$" || true)"
    38	if [ -z "${ALLOW_DIRTY:-}" ] && [ -n "$dirty" ]; then
    39	  echo "refusing to submit from a dirty tree (set ALLOW_DIRTY=1 to override):" >&2
    40	  echo "$dirty" >&2
    41	  exit 1
    42	fi
    43	
    44	# JAX_* env leaks into train tasks via dispatch forwarding, and this fork enables pipelined host
    45	# offloading at optimization level O1+ regardless of its flag -- a leaked level would silently
    46	# turn a control into an H10 treatment.
    47	if [ -n "${JAX_OPTIMIZATION_LEVEL:-}" ]; then
    48	  echo "JAX_OPTIMIZATION_LEVEL is set (${JAX_OPTIMIZATION_LEVEL}); unset it -- it changes compiler passes behind the arms' backs" >&2
    49	  exit 1
    50	fi
    51	
    52	# A flag-based treatment must be deliberate: an ARM_XLA_FLAGS value leaking from the supervisor
    53	# shell into a "control" draw silently runs the treatment and records nothing -- the null delta
    54	# then reads as "dead lever". Controls run with TREATMENT unset and ARM_XLA_FLAGS empty.
    55	if [ -n "${ARM_XLA_FLAGS:-}" ] && [ -z "${TREATMENT:-}" ]; then
    56	  echo "ARM_XLA_FLAGS is set but TREATMENT=1 is not; refusing (is this a contaminated control?)" >&2
    57	  exit 1
    58	fi
    59	if [ -n "${DK_CTAS_PER_SM:-}" ] && [ -z "${TREATMENT:-}" ]; then
    60	  echo "DK_CTAS_PER_SM is set but TREATMENT=1 is not; refusing (is this a contaminated control?)" >&2
    61	  exit 1
    62	fi
    63	
    64	# One RID/VERSION per submission, ever: a reused RID merges W&B histories, resurrects the
    65	# leader-populated compile cache (clique-deadlock recipe), and can vacuously reuse artifacts.
    66	if [ -f "${LOOP_DIR}/arms.tsv" ] && awk -F'\t' -v rid="$RID" -v ver="$VERSION" \
    67	    'NR>1 && ($1==rid || $3==ver) {found=1} END {exit !found}' "${LOOP_DIR}/arms.tsv"; then
    68	  echo "RID ${RID} or VERSION ${VERSION} already appears in arms.tsv; pick fresh ones" >&2
    69	  exit 1
    70	fi
    71	
    72	PRIORITY="${PRIORITY:-production}"
    73	# Covers Kueue queue wait PLUS the run: same-band gangs queue FIFO behind whatever holds the
    74	# rack, and the coordinator's timeout clock runs while its child sits in the gate. Occupancy
    75	# after admission is intrinsically bounded by NUM_STEPS (~30 steps past restore); the watchdog
    76	# enforces the <1h-runtime rule from admission.
    77	ARM_TIMEOUT="${ARM_TIMEOUT:-28800}"
    78	NUM_STEPS="${NUM_STEPS:-30030}"
    79	TRAINING_DATA="${TRAINING_DATA:-mixture}"
    80	MASTER_PARAMS="${MASTER_PARAMS:-device}"
    81	MOE_IMPL="${MOE_IMPL:-ragged_all_to_all}"
    82	RESTORE_FROM="${RESTORE_FROM:-s3://marin-us-east-02a/marin/grug/hero-12d8b6f0-dee637/2026.08.19.2/checkpoints/step-30000}"
    83	RESTORE_ARGS=()
    84	if [ -n "$RESTORE_FROM" ]; then
    85	  RESTORE_ARGS=(--restore-from "$RESTORE_FROM")
    86	fi
    87	# The learning-rate schedule's length; fixed across arms so losses stay comparable.
    88	SCHEDULE_STEPS=4470000
    89	
    90	read -r -a EXTRA <<<"${EXTRA_LAUNCH_ARGS:-}"
    91	
    92	cd "$REPO"
    93	uv run iris --config lib/iris/config/marin.yaml job run \
    94	  --no-wait --enable-extra-resources \
    95	  --target-cluster cw-us-east-08a --priority "${PRIORITY}" \
    96	  --cpu 2 --memory 8GB --disk 32GB --timeout "${ARM_TIMEOUT}" \
    97	  --job-name "${RID}-coord" \
    98	  -e IRIS_USER mwittmann \
    99	  -e WANDB_API_KEY "${WANDB_API_KEY}" \
   100	  -e WANDB_PROJECT marin_moe \
   101	  -e MARIN_PREFIX s3://marin-us-east-02a/marin \
   102	  -e IRIS_PORT_JAX 32703 \
   103	  -e AWS_MAX_ATTEMPTS 25 -e AWS_RETRY_MODE adaptive \
   104	  -e JAX_COMPILATION_CACHE_DIR "s3://marin-us-east-02a/marin/tmp/ttl=30d/jaxcache/${RID}" \
   105	  -e XLA_FLAGS "${ARM_XLA_FLAGS:-}" \
   106	  -e XLA_PYTHON_CLIENT_MEM_FRACTION "${MEM_FRACTION:-0.75}" \
   107	  -e XLA_RAGGED_A2A_DK_CTAS_PER_SM "${DK_CTAS_PER_SM:-}" \
   108	  -e TF_CPP_MIN_LOG_LEVEL 0 \
   109	  -e TF_CPP_VMODULE "hlo_rematerialization=1,execution_stream_assignment=1,collective_pipeliner=1,ragged_all_to_all_thunk=3" \
   110	  -- python -m experiments.grug.moe_hero_ep.launch_diagnostics \
   111	     --run-id "${RID}" \
   112	     --dp-racks 1 --num-steps "${NUM_STEPS}" --schedule-steps "${SCHEDULE_STEPS}" \
   113	     --capacity-factor 1.15 \
   114	     --moe-implementation "${MOE_IMPL}" \
   115	     --processes-per-task 4 \
   116	     --master-params "${MASTER_PARAMS}" \
   117	     --training-data "${TRAINING_DATA}" \
   118	     --profile-steps "${PROFILE_STEPS:-0}" --profile-start-step "${PROFILE_START_STEP:-30021}" \
   119	     --watch-interval 0 --eval-every 0 \
   120	     --no-save-checkpoints \
   121	     "${RESTORE_ARGS[@]}" \
   122	     "${EXTRA[@]}" \
   123	     --version "${VERSION}" --run >"${LOOP_DIR}/${RID}-submit.log" 2>&1 \
   124	  || { echo "submit FAILED, tail of ${RID}-submit.log:" >&2; tail -20 "${LOOP_DIR}/${RID}-submit.log" >&2; exit 1; }
   125	
   126	printf '%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\n' \
   127	  "${RID}" "$(git -C "$REPO" rev-parse --short HEAD)" "${VERSION}" "${MOE_IMPL}" "${TRAINING_DATA}" \
   128	  "${MASTER_PARAMS}" "${SCHEDULE_STEPS}" "${ARM_TIMEOUT}" "${RESTORE_FROM:-none}" \
   129	  "${EXTRA_LAUNCH_ARGS:-none}" "${ARM_XLA_FLAGS:-none}" "${MEM_FRACTION:-0.75}" "${DK_CTAS_PER_SM:-none}" >> "${LOOP_DIR}/arms.tsv"
   130	
   131	echo "submitted ${RID} at ${PRIORITY} priority (timeout ${ARM_TIMEOUT}s, commit $(git -C "$REPO" rev-parse --short HEAD), data ${TRAINING_DATA}, extra: ${EXTRA_LAUNCH_ARGS:-none}, xla_flags: ${ARM_XLA_FLAGS:-none}, memfrac: ${MEM_FRACTION:-0.75}, dk_ctas: ${DK_CTAS_PER_SM:-none})"
     1	# Copyright The Marin Authors
     2	# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
     3	
     4	"""Score one arm of the ragged EP64 tuning loop from its W&B history.
     5	
     6	The primary metric is the run MEDIAN of ``throughput/mfu`` over a fixed step window (sparse
     7	environmental stall steps dominate means; see DESIGN "Metric and guards"); the mean and a stall
     8	count are emitted alongside. The window is fixed for the whole loop: comparing arms scored over
     9	different windows compares warmup fractions, not transports.
    10	
    11	Also emits the two guard quantities that come from the same run -- the worst drop fraction and the
    12	loss at the window's last step -- so a faster arm that quietly dropped tokens or changed the model
    13	cannot be recorded as an improvement.
    14	
    15	usage: uv run --with wandb python score.py <run-id> [--lo 5] [--hi 19]
    16	"""
    17	
    18	import argparse
    19	import json
    20	import math
    21	import statistics
    22	import sys
    23	
    24	import wandb
    25	
    26	MFU_KEY = "throughput/mfu"
    27	LOSS_KEY = "train/loss"
    28	DROP_KEY = "moe/drop_fraction"
    29	TOKENS_KEY = "throughput/tokens_per_second"
    30	PEAK_KEY = "memory/peak_gib"
    31	
    32	
    33	def main() -> None:
    34	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    35	    parser.add_argument("run_id")
    36	    parser.add_argument("--lo", type=int, default=5, help="first scored step")
    37	    parser.add_argument("--hi", type=int, default=19, help="last scored step")
    38	    parser.add_argument("--project", default="marin-community/marin_moe")
    39	    parser.add_argument(
    40	        "--relative",
    41	        action="store_true",
    42	        help="Read lo/hi as offsets from the run's first logged step. A restored run resumes at "
    43	        "the checkpoint's step, so an absolute window would miss it entirely.",
    44	    )
    45	    parser.add_argument(
    46	        "--expect-min-first-step",
    47	        type=int,
    48	        default=30000,
    49	        help="Fail unless the run's first logged step is at least this. A restore that silently "
    50	        "fell back to scratch logs from step 1 and would otherwise score as a valid window.",
    51	    )
    52	    args = parser.parse_args()
    53	
    54	    run = wandb.Api().run(f"{args.project}/{args.run_id}")
    55	    rows = list(run.scan_history())
    56	    steps = [int(r["_step"]) for r in rows if r.get("_step") is not None]
    57	    by_step: dict[int, dict] = {}
    58	    for row in rows:
    59	        step = row.get("_step")
    60	        if step is None:
    61	            continue
    62	        by_step.setdefault(int(step), {}).update({k: v for k, v in row.items() if v is not None})
    63	
    64	    first_step = min(by_step) if by_step else 0
    65	    lo, hi = (first_step + args.lo, first_step + args.hi) if args.relative else (args.lo, args.hi)
    66	
    67	    def series(key: str) -> list[float]:
    68	        return [float(by_step[s][key]) for s in sorted(by_step) if lo <= s <= hi and key in by_step[s]]
    69	
    70	    mfu = series(MFU_KEY)
    71	    drops = series(DROP_KEY)
    72	    peaks = series(PEAK_KEY)
    73	    losses = [(s, by_step[s][LOSS_KEY]) for s in sorted(by_step) if lo <= s <= hi and LOSS_KEY in by_step[s]]
    74	    tokens = series(TOKENS_KEY)
    75	
    76	    result = {
    77	        "run_id": args.run_id,
    78	        "state": run.state,
    79	        "max_step": max(steps) if steps else -1,
    80	        "window": [lo, hi],
    81	        "first_step": first_step,
    82	        "scored_points": len(mfu),
    83	        # A window with holes in it is not the window the loop agreed to compare, so say so loudly
    84	        # rather than averaging whatever happens to be there.
    85	        "window_complete": len(mfu) == hi - lo + 1,
    86	        # The PRIMARY decision statistic (DESIGN, calibrated at iteration 0): sparse environmental
    87	        # stall steps swing the mean by >2x the keep bar while medians hold; stall count guards
    88	        # the median's blind spot (a treatment that CAUSES stalls must not hide behind it).
    89	        "mfu_median": round(statistics.median(mfu), 4) if mfu else None,
    90	        "stall_steps": (
    91	            sum(1 for v in mfu if v < statistics.median(mfu) * 0.97) if mfu else None
    92	        ),
    93	        "mfu_mean": round(statistics.fmean(mfu), 4) if mfu else None,
    94	        "mfu_stdev": round(statistics.stdev(mfu), 4) if len(mfu) > 1 else None,
    95	        "tokens_per_second_mean": round(statistics.fmean(tokens), 1) if tokens else None,
    96	        # Drops are an outcome here, not only a guard: the whole point of the ragged transport is
    97	        # moving tokens the pooled one clips, so a throughput win that costs drops is not a win.
    98	        # Engagement/confound telemetry: HloRematerialization flips on allocator-limit crossings
    99	        # (issue #8054's +9.08% artifact); a memory-moving arm whose peak did not move did not
   100	        # engage, and an MFU delta with a big peak move may be a remat-boundary crossing instead.
   101	        "peak_gib_max": round(max(peaks), 2) if peaks else None,
   102	        "drop_fraction_max": max(drops) if drops else None,
   103	        "drop_fraction_mean": round(statistics.fmean(drops), 6) if drops else None,
   104	        # Guard-series completeness: a killed run can leave the last rows of less-frequent keys
   105	        # unflushed, so a "last loss" alone can silently come from an earlier step. Emit the full
   106	        # series; the loop compares max |delta| against the paired control (data is deterministic
   107	        # across arms at a fixed restore step, so the series pair pointwise).
   108	        "drop_points": len(drops),
   109	        "loss_points": len(losses),
   110	        "loss_series": [[s, round(float(v), 6)] for s, v in losses],
   111	        "loss_last": float(losses[-1][1]) if losses else None,
   112	        "loss_last_step": losses[-1][0] if losses else None,
   113	    }
   114	
   115	    # One enforced verdict instead of a pile of fields a tired reader can skim past. Anything
   116	    # that makes the window not the window the loop agreed to compare -- a scratch fallback, a
   117	    # hole in any guard series, a NaN -- fails the arm here, not downstream.
   118	    expected_points = hi - lo + 1
   119	    numeric = [x for x in (mfu + drops + [v for _, v in losses] + peaks) if x is not None]
   120	    problems = []
   121	    if first_step < args.expect_min_first_step:
   122	        problems.append(f"first_step {first_step} < {args.expect_min_first_step}: restore fell back to scratch")
   123	    if len(mfu) != expected_points:
   124	        problems.append(f"mfu series has {len(mfu)}/{expected_points} points")
   125	    if len(drops) != expected_points:
   126	        problems.append(f"drop series has {len(drops)}/{expected_points} points")
   127	    if len(losses) != expected_points:
   128	        problems.append(f"loss series has {len(losses)}/{expected_points} points")
   129	    if losses and losses[-1][0] != hi:
   130	        problems.append(f"last loss is from step {losses[-1][0]}, not window end {hi}")
   131	    if any(not math.isfinite(float(x)) for x in numeric):
   132	        problems.append("non-finite value in a scored series")
   133	    result["valid"] = not problems
   134	    result["problems"] = problems
   135	    print(json.dumps(result, indent=2))
   136	    if problems:
   137	        sys.exit(1)
   138	
   139	
   140	if __name__ == "__main__":
   141	    main()

    49	
    50	def compute_instant_throughput(
    51	    batch_size: int,
    52	    step_duration: float,
    53	    tokens_per_example: int,
    54	    flops_per_example: float | None = None,
    55	    theoretical_flops: float | None = None,
    56	) -> InstantThroughput:
    57	    """Compute examples/sec, tokens/sec, model FLOPs/sec, and MFU for one step."""
    58	    if step_duration <= 0.0:
    59	        return InstantThroughput()
    60	    examples_per_second = batch_size / step_duration
    61	    tokens_per_second = tokens_per_example * examples_per_second
    62	    model_flops_per_second = mfu = None
    63	    if flops_per_example is not None:
    64	        model_flops_per_second = flops_per_example / step_duration * batch_size
    65	        if theoretical_flops is not None:
    66	            mfu = model_flops_per_second / theoretical_flops * 100.0
    67	    return InstantThroughput(examples_per_second, tokens_per_second, model_flops_per_second, mfu)
    68	
    69	
    70	def log_step_info(total_steps: Optional[int], batch_schedule: Optional[BatchSchedule] = None):
    71	    def log_step_info_inner(step: StepInfo):
    72	        metrics = {"train/loss": step.loss, "global_step": step.step}
    73	        if total_steps:
    74	            if batch_schedule is not None:
    75	                total_examples = batch_schedule.global_data_offset_by_step(total_steps)
    76	                current_examples = batch_schedule.global_data_offset_by_step(step.step + 1)
    77	                metrics["run_progress"] = current_examples / total_examples
    78	            else:
    79	                metrics["run_progress"] = step.step / total_steps
    80	        log_optimizer_hyperparams(step.opt_state, step=step.step, prefix="optim")
    81	        levanter.tracker.log(metrics, step=step.step)
    82	
    83	    return log_step_info_inner
    84	
    85	
    86	def log_performance_stats(
    87	    tokens_per_example: int,
    88	    batch_schedule: int | BatchSchedule,
    89	    flops_per_example: Optional[float] = None,
    90	    prefix: Optional[str] = "throughput",
    91	):
    92	    if isinstance(batch_schedule, int):
    93	        batch_schedule = BatchSchedule(batch_schedule)
    94	
    95	    def wrap_key(key):
    96	        if prefix:
    97	            return f"{prefix}/{key}"
    98	        return key
    99	
   100	    device_count = jax.device_count()
   101	    device = jax.devices()[0]
   102	
   103	    flops_per_device = device_flops_for_jax_device(device.device_kind)
   104	    theoretical_flops = flops_per_device * device_count if flops_per_device is not None else None
   105	    levanter.tracker.log_summary(
   106	        {
   107	            wrap_key("device_kind"): device.device_kind,
   108	        }
   109	    )
   110	
   111	    if flops_per_device is not None:
   112	        levanter.tracker.log_summary(
   113	            {
   114	                wrap_key("theoretical_flops_per_device"): flops_per_device,
   115	                wrap_key("theoretical_flops"): theoretical_flops,
   116	            }
   117	        )
   118	
   119	    if flops_per_example is not None:
   120	        levanter.tracker.log_summary({wrap_key("flops_per_example"): flops_per_example})
   121	
   122	    # Accumulate MFU samples over a trailing window for robust distribution stats.
   123	    # 500 steps is large enough for stable percentiles while bounded in memory.
   124	    mfu_window: collections.deque[float] = collections.deque(maxlen=500)
   125	
   126	    def log_performance_stats(step_info: StepInfo):
   127	        dict_to_log: dict[str, float | int] = {}
   128	
   129	        # log these totals because it's useful for comparing different seqlens, batch sizes, etc
   130	        # TODO: if we add seqlen schedules this will get even more complex
   131	        this_batch_size = batch_schedule.batch_size_at_step(step_info.step)
   132	        total_examples = batch_schedule.global_data_offset_by_step(step_info.step + 1)
   133	        total_tokens = tokens_per_example * total_examples
   134	        dict_to_log["total_tokens"] = total_tokens
   135	
   136	        if flops_per_example:
   137	            total_flops = flops_per_example * total_examples
   138	            dict_to_log["total_gflops"] = total_flops / 1e9
   139	
   140	        throughput = compute_instant_throughput(
   141	            this_batch_size, step_info.step_duration, tokens_per_example, flops_per_example, theoretical_flops
   142	        )
   143	        if throughput.examples_per_second is not None and throughput.tokens_per_second is not None:
   144	            dict_to_log["examples_per_second"] = throughput.examples_per_second
   145	            dict_to_log["tokens_per_second"] = throughput.tokens_per_second
   146	            dict_to_log["duration"] = step_info.step_duration
   147	        if throughput.model_flops_per_second is not None:
   148	            dict_to_log["gflops_per_second"] = throughput.model_flops_per_second / 1e9
   149	        if throughput.mfu is not None:
   150	            dict_to_log["mfu"] = throughput.mfu
   151	            mfu_window.append(throughput.mfu)
   152	
   153	        dict_to_log = {wrap_key(k): v for k, v in dict_to_log.items()}
   154	        levanter.tracker.log(dict_to_log, step=step_info.step)
   155	
   156	        if len(mfu_window) > 0:
   157	            n = len(mfu_window)
   158	            mean = statistics.mean(mfu_window)
   159	            med = statistics.median(mfu_window)
   160	            if n >= 2:
   161	                deciles = statistics.quantiles(mfu_window, n=10)
   162	                p10, p90 = deciles[0], deciles[8]
   163	                sd = statistics.stdev(mfu_window)
   164	            else:
   165	                p10 = p90 = mfu_window[0]
   166	                sd = 0.0
   167	            levanter.tracker.log_summary(
   168	                {
   169	                    wrap_key("p10_mfu"): p10,
   170	                    wrap_key("p50_mfu"): med,
   171	                    wrap_key("p90_mfu"): p90,
   172	                    wrap_key("mean_mfu"): mean,
   173	                    wrap_key("stddev_mfu"): sd,
   174	                    wrap_key("mfu_sample_count"): n,
   175	                }
   176	            )
   177	
   178	    return log_performance_stats
   179

    70	HERO_EP_RUNTIME_ENV = {
    71	    "LD_PRELOAD": "libjemalloc.so.2",
    72	    "MALLOC_CONF": "background_thread:true,dirty_decay_ms:0,muzzy_decay_ms:0,narenas:2",
    73	    "JAX_ENABLE_PGLE": "false",
    74	    "XLA_PJRT_GPU_HOST_MEMORY_LIMIT_GB": "192",
    75	    "XLA_PYTHON_CLIENT_ALLOCATOR": "cuda_async",
    76	    # `cuda_async` reads this fraction as the mempool release threshold, and PJRT sizes the
    77	    # collective pool at the remaining `(1 - fraction) x 184.3 GiB`. 0.75 sets a 138.2 GiB
    78	    # threshold and leaves ~46 GiB for collectives, above the ~28.5 GiB NCCL, cuBLAS, and the
    79	    # CUDA context hold outside the allocator. A higher fraction strands memory the step never
    80	    # uses, because the startup preallocation probe commits the whole threshold at once: 0.83
    81	    # pins 153.0 GiB in the pool and leaves under 3 GiB free on the device, so the arena
    82	    # allocation below has no room to remap into.
    83	    "XLA_PYTHON_CLIENT_MEM_FRACTION": "0.75",
    84	}
    85	XLA_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_FLAG = "--xla_gpu_experimental_parallel_collective_overlap_limit"
    86	DEFAULT_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT = 4
    87	DEFAULT_DROPLESS_MOE_IMPLEMENTATION: MoeImplementation = "sonic_cute"
    88	# Full inline norm watch failed with overlap 4. Overlap 1 completed the selected full-watch gate.
    89	INLINE_WATCH_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT = 1
    90	# The ragged transport wants the opposite scheduling posture from the fixed and pooled ones. Its
   135	
   136	    DONATED donates the whole train state into the step, the original behavior. EXCLUDED passes
   137	    the resident momentum leaves as a separate, non-donated argument, so XLA can neither alias
   138	    them to an output nor reuse their buffers as scratch, and adds a per-step canary checksum of
   139	    each resident leaf (``debug/resident_ck_in`` / ``debug/resident_ck_out``). This discriminates
   140	    the k=1 NaN hypotheses: S1 (donation-enabled buffer reuse clobbers the leaf under the async
   141	    offload schedule) from S2 (a stray transport write lands in the leaf's address range
   142	    regardless of donation). EXCLUDED costs one extra device copy of each resident leaf per step
   143	    (the un-donated input and the fresh output coexist: +10.87 GiB transient per leaf at d6144
   144	    EP64, predicted peak ~134 GiB at k=1 -- near the 138.2 GiB threshold; watch for #8490
   145	    allocator churn).
   146	    """
   147	
   148	    DONATED = "donated"
   149	    EXCLUDED = "excluded"
   150	
   151	
   152	def restore_template_from(state):
   153	    """ShapeDtypeStructs carrying each leaf's concrete sharding, releasing the leaves.
   154	
   155	    `jax.eval_shape` drops the `pinned_host` memory kind that `initial_state` puts on
   900	
   901	
   902	def _make_diagnostic_watch_step(mp: jmp.Policy, *, z_loss_weight: float, watch_config: WatchConfig):
   903	    watch_targets = (
   904	        tuple(t.strip() for t in watch_config.watch_targets.split(","))
   905	        if isinstance(watch_config.watch_targets, str)
   906	        else tuple(watch_config.watch_targets)
   907	    )
   908	    unsupported_targets = set(watch_targets) - {"grads", "params"}
   909	    if unsupported_targets:
   910	        raise ValueError(f"diagnostic watch does not support targets {sorted(unsupported_targets)}")
   911	    diagnostic_watch_config = replace(watch_config, watch_targets=list(watch_targets))
   912	    z_loss = z_loss_weight if z_loss_weight > 0 else None
   913	
   914	    @jax.jit
   915	    def diagnostic_watch_step(params: Transformer, batch, pending_qb_betas: jax.Array):
   916	        params = _apply_qb_betas(params, pending_qb_betas)
   917	        return _compute_diagnostic_watch_stats(params, batch, mp, z_loss, diagnostic_watch_config)
   918	
   919	    return diagnostic_watch_step
   920	
   921	
   922	def _make_train_step(
   923	    optimizer: optax.GradientTransformation,
   924	    mp: jmp.Policy,
   925	    *,
   926	    z_loss_weight: float,
   927	    ema_beta: float | None,
   928	    watch_config: WatchConfig | None = None,
   929	    offload_opt_state: bool = False,
   930	    opt_state_resident_expert_leaves: int = 0,
   931	    opt_state_resident_donation: ResidentDonation = ResidentDonation.DONATED,
   932	    master_param_mode: MasterParamMode = MasterParamMode.DEVICE,
   933	):
   934	    if opt_state_resident_expert_leaves and not offload_opt_state:
   935	        raise ValueError("opt_state_resident_expert_leaves requires offload_opt_state=True")
   936	    resident_excluded = opt_state_resident_donation is ResidentDonation.EXCLUDED
   937	    if resident_excluded and not opt_state_resident_expert_leaves:
   938	        raise ValueError("opt_state_resident_donation=excluded requires opt_state_resident_expert_leaves > 0")
   939	    one = jnp.array(1, dtype=jnp.int32)
   940	    z_loss = z_loss_weight if z_loss_weight > 0 else None
   941	    if watch_config is not None:
   942	        if isinstance(watch_config.watch_targets, str):
   943	            watch_targets = tuple(t.strip() for t in watch_config.watch_targets.split(","))
   944	        else:
   945	            watch_targets = tuple(watch_config.watch_targets)
   946	    else:
   947	        watch_targets = ()
   948	
   949	    def train_step(state: GrugTrainState, batch):
   950	        # Apply pending QB betas to router biases inside JIT (avoids eager
   951	        # host-side TPU kernel launches that can cause SPMD sync issues).
   952	        qb_params = _apply_qb_betas(state.params, state.pending_qb_betas)
   953	        if ema_beta is not None:
   954	            qb_ema_params = _apply_qb_betas(state.ema_params, state.pending_qb_betas)
   955	        else:
   956	            qb_ema_params = None
   957	
   958	        (loss, summarized_metrics), grads = _loss_and_grads(qb_params, batch, mp, z_loss)
   959	        metrics = {"train/loss": loss, **summarized_metrics}
   960	        if offload_opt_state:
   961	            # Resident leaves already live on device; skipping them here and below is what keeps
   962	            # them out of the per-step host round-trip. `optimizer.update` preserves the state
   963	            # structure, so the input mask names the same leaves in the updated state.
   964	            opt_offload_mask = _opt_state_offload_mask(state.opt_state, opt_state_resident_expert_leaves)
   965	            opt_state_in = _opt_state_to_memory_kind(state.opt_state, "device", opt_offload_mask)
   966	        else:
   967	            opt_offload_mask = None
   968	            opt_state_in = state.opt_state
   969	        if master_param_mode == MasterParamMode.FP32_PINNED_HOST:
   970	            if state.master_params is None:
   971	                raise ValueError("master_params must be initialized for an FP32 pinned-host master.")
   972	            master_params_in = _tree_to_memory_kind(state.master_params, "device")
   973	            master_params_in = _apply_qb_betas(master_params_in, state.pending_qb_betas)
   974	            master_grads = _FP32_POLICY.cast_to_param(grads)
   975	            updates, opt_state = optimizer.update(master_grads, opt_state_in, master_params_in)
   976	            master_params = optax.apply_updates(master_params_in, updates)
   977	            params = mp.cast_to_param(master_params)
   978	            master_params = _tree_to_memory_kind(master_params, "pinned_host")
   979	        else:
   980	            updates, opt_state = optimizer.update(grads, opt_state_in, qb_params)
   981	            params = optax.apply_updates(qb_params, updates)
   982	            master_params = None
   983	
   984	        if resident_excluded:
   985	            # Canary: checksum of each resident momentum leaf as read by this step (ck_in) and as
   986	            # produced by it (ck_out). ck_in[t+1] != ck_out[t] is strong evidence of a stray
   987	            # BETWEEN-step write (S2). Matching checksums while training still corrupts mean an
   988	            # in-step external write or a generic bug -- NOT S1: donation is off in this mode, so
   989	            # the S1 verdict can only come from the arm-level outcome (excluded runs clean), and
   990	            # even that is suggestive rather than conclusive, since exclusion relocates the leaf.
   991	            metrics.update(_resident_canary_metrics("debug/resident_ck_in", state.opt_state, opt_offload_mask))
   992	            metrics.update(_resident_canary_metrics("debug/resident_ck_out", opt_state, opt_offload_mask))
   993	
   994	        if ema_beta is None:
   995	            ema_params = None
   996	        else:
   997	            if qb_ema_params is None:
   998	                raise ValueError("ema_params must be initialized when ema_beta is set.")
   999	            ema_params = jax.tree_util.tree_map(
  1000	                lambda old, new: ema_beta * old + (1.0 - ema_beta) * new,
  1001	                qb_ema_params,
  1002	                params,
  1003	            )
  1004	
  1005	        watch_stats = None
  1006	        if watch_config is not None:
  1007	            watch_stats = compute_watch_stats(
  1008	                watch_targets=watch_targets,
  1009	                include_norms=watch_config.include_norms,
  1010	                include_per_parameter_norms=watch_config.include_per_parameter_norms,
  1011	                include_histogram=watch_config.include_histograms,
  1012	                split_scan_layers=watch_config.split_scan_layers,
  1013	                params=qb_params,
  1014	                grads=grads,
  1015	                updates=updates,
  1016	                opt_state=opt_state_in,
  1017	                model_tree_type=type(state.params),
  1018	            )
  1019	
  1020	        if offload_opt_state:
  1021	            opt_state = _opt_state_to_memory_kind(opt_state, "pinned_host", opt_offload_mask)
  1022	
  1023	        next_state = dataclasses.replace(
  1024	            state,
  1025	            step=state.step + one,
  1026	            params=params,
  1027	            master_params=master_params,
  1028	            opt_state=opt_state,
  1029	            ema_params=ema_params,
  1030	            pending_qb_betas=metrics["qb_beta_per_layer"],
  1031	        )
  1032	
  1033	        return next_state, metrics, watch_stats
  1034	
  1035	    if not resident_excluded:
  1036	        return jax.jit(train_step, donate_argnums=(0,))
  1037	
  1038	    @functools.partial(jax.jit, donate_argnums=(0,))
  1039	    def resident_excluded_train_step(state: GrugTrainState, resident_opt_leaves, batch):
  1040	        merged = dataclasses.replace(state, opt_state=eqx.combine(state.opt_state, resident_opt_leaves))
  1041	        return train_step(merged, batch)
  1042	
  1043	    return _ResidentExcludedTrainStep(resident_excluded_train_step, opt_state_resident_expert_leaves)
  1044	
  1045	
  1046	class _ResidentExcludedTrainStep:
  1047	    """Train step that passes the resident momentum leaves as a separate, non-donated argument.
  1048	
  1049	    Donation is per-argument, so splitting the resident leaves out of the donated state is what
  1050	    removes them from XLA's input/output alias set; ``jit_train_step`` is exposed so tests and
  1051	    diagnostics can inspect the lowering's aliasing directly.
  1052	    """
  1053	
  1054	    def __init__(self, jit_train_step, resident_expert_leaves: int):
  1055	        self.jit_train_step = jit_train_step
  1056	        self._resident_expert_leaves = resident_expert_leaves
  1057	
  1058	    def __call__(self, state: GrugTrainState, batch):
  1059	        offloaded_opt, resident_opt = _split_resident_opt_state(state.opt_state, self._resident_expert_leaves)
  1060	        donated = dataclasses.replace(state, opt_state=offloaded_opt)
  1061	        return self.jit_train_step(donated, resident_opt, batch)
  1062	
  1063	
  1064	def _run_grug_local(config: GrugRunConfig) -> None:
  1065	    """Entry point for the grug template training loop."""
  1066	    if config.model.moe_implementation == RAGGED_MOE_IMPLEMENTATION:
  1067	        verify_ragged_pjrt()
  1068	    if config.tensorstore_cache_bytes is not None:
  1069	        set_jagged_array_read_cache_bytes(config.tensorstore_cache_bytes)
  1070	
  1071	    trainer = config.trainer.trainer
  1072	    trainer.initialize()
  1073	    levanter.tracker.log_configuration(config)
  1074	
  1075	    run_id = trainer.id
  1076	    if run_id is None:
  1077	        raise ValueError("trainer.id was not initialized")
  1078	
  1079	    optimizer = config.optimizer.build(trainer.num_train_steps)
  1080	    watch_config = trainer.watch
  1081	    diagnostic_watch_step = None
  1082	    inline_watch_config = watch_config if watch_config.is_enabled else None
  1083	    if watch_config.is_enabled and config.trainer.watch_mode == WatchMode.DIAGNOSTIC:
  1084	        diagnostic_watch_step = _make_diagnostic_watch_step(
  1085	            trainer.mp,
  1086	            z_loss_weight=config.trainer.z_loss_weight,
  1087	            watch_config=watch_config,
  1088	        )
  1089	        inline_watch_config = None
  1090	    train_step = _make_train_step(
  1091	        optimizer,
  1092	        trainer.mp,
  1093	        z_loss_weight=config.trainer.z_loss_weight,
  1094	        ema_beta=config.trainer.ema_beta,
  1095	        watch_config=inline_watch_config,
  1096	        offload_opt_state=config.trainer.offload_opt_state,
  1097	        opt_state_resident_expert_leaves=config.trainer.opt_state_resident_expert_leaves,
  1098	        opt_state_resident_donation=config.trainer.opt_state_resident_donation,
  1099	        master_param_mode=config.trainer.master_param_mode,
  1100	    )
  1101	
  1102	    data_key, model_key = jax.random.split(jax.random.PRNGKey(trainer.seed), 2)
  1103	    if config.trainer.data_seed is not None:
  1104	        data_key = jax.random.PRNGKey(config.trainer.data_seed)
  1105	
  1106	    # Grug uses raw PartitionSpecs rather than Trainer's logical axis mapping.
  1107	    # Keep the mesh compact so the batch pspec derived by `_batch_spec(mesh)` spans slices directly.
  1108	    # replica_axis_size=None lets compact_grug_mesh default to jax.process_count() (full
  1109	    # cross-slice replication); set it to 1 on GrugTrainerConfig for cross-slice FSDP.
  1110	    mesh = compact_grug_mesh(
  1320	                # starts above step 1, thus it never fires. The hooks log at `StepInfo.step`, which
  1321	                # is 0 there, so the point lands at step 0 on the curve.
  1322	                if eval_cfg.eval_at_first_step:
  1323	                    for hook in eval_hooks:
  1324	                        state_callbacks.add_hook(_first_step_only(hook), every=1)
  1325	
  1326	        last_loss: float | jax.Array = 0.0
  1327	        last_step_duration = 0.0
  1328	        # Host-side memory of the previous step's resident-leaf checksums (excluded-donation
  1329	        # canary): a ck_in that differs from the prior ck_out means the buffer changed between
  1330	        # steps.
  1331	        previous_resident_ck_out: dict[str, int] = {}
  1332	
  1333	        # Main optimization loop.
  1334	        try:
  1335	            while int(state.step) < stop_step:
  1336	                with jax.profiler.TraceAnnotation("load_batch"):
  1337	                    batch = next(iterator)
  1338	                current_step = int(state.step)
  1339	                watch_due = (
  1340	                    watch_config.is_enabled and watch_config.interval > 0 and current_step % watch_config.interval == 0
  1341	                )
  1342	                if watch_due and diagnostic_watch_step is not None:
  1343	                    watch_stats = diagnostic_watch_step(state.params, batch, state.pending_qb_betas)
  1344	                    jax.block_until_ready(watch_stats)
  1345	                else:
  1346	                    watch_stats = None
  1347	                step_start = time.perf_counter()
  1348	                state_callbacks.emit_event(callbacks.ProgressEvent.TRAIN_STEP_STARTED)
  1349	                state, metrics, inline_watch_stats = train_step(state, batch)
  1350	                if inline_watch_stats is not None and watch_due:
  1351	                    watch_stats = inline_watch_stats
  1352	                step = int(state.step) - 1
  1353	
  1354	                jax.block_until_ready(metrics["train/loss"])
  1355	                state_callbacks.emit_event(callbacks.ProgressEvent.TRAIN_STEP_FINISHED)
  1356	
  1357	                if not jnp.isfinite(metrics["train/loss"]):
  1358	                    # The NaN step's canary is the single most discriminating datum for the
  1359	                    # resident-leaf corruption investigation; surface it before dying.
  1360	                    nan_canary = {
  1361	                        key: int(value) for key, value in metrics.items() if key.startswith("debug/resident_ck_")
  1362	                    }
  1363	                    canary_note = f" resident canary: {nan_canary} previous_out: {previous_resident_ck_out}" if nan_canary else ""
  1364	                    raise RuntimeError(
  1365	                        f"Non-finite loss ({float(metrics['train/loss'])}) at step {int(state.step)}.{canary_note}"
  1366	                    )
  1367	                duration = time.perf_counter() - step_start
  1368	                hook_start = time.perf_counter()
  1369	                with jax.profiler.TraceAnnotation("callbacks"):
  1370	                    state_callbacks.run(state, loss=metrics["train/loss"], step_duration=duration)
  1371	                    last_loss = metrics["train/loss"]
  1372	                    last_step_duration = duration
  1373	                    levanter.tracker.log({"throughput/hook_time": time.perf_counter() - hook_start}, step=step)
  1374	                    levanter.tracker.log({"throughput/loading_time": iterator.this_load_time}, step=step)
  1375	                    router_metrics = {
  1376	                        key: value
  1377	                        for key, value in metrics.items()
  1378	                        if (key.startswith("train/router/") or key.startswith("moe_bias/"))
  1379	                        and key not in ("train/router/routing_counts_per_layer", "qb_beta_per_layer")
  1380	                    }
  1381	                    if router_metrics:
  1382	                        levanter.tracker.log(router_metrics, step=step)
  1383	                    if "train/cross_entropy_loss" in metrics:
  1384	                        levanter.tracker.log(
  1385	                            {"train/cross_entropy_loss": metrics["train/cross_entropy_loss"]},
  1386	                            step=step,
  1387	                        )
  1388	                    if "moe/dropped_assignments" in metrics:
  1389	                        drop_metrics = _drop_metrics(
  1390	                            metrics["moe/dropped_assignments"],
  1391	                            metrics["moe/sender_dropped_assignments"],
  1392	                            metrics["moe/receiver_dropped_assignments"],
  1393	                            batch_size=batch.tokens.shape[0],
  1394	                            sequence_length=batch.tokens.shape[1],
  1395	                            top_k=config.model.num_experts_per_token,
  1396	                            num_layers=config.model.num_layers,
  1397	                        )
  1398	                        levanter.tracker.log(drop_metrics, step=step)
  1399	
  1400	                    resident_canary = {
  1401	                        key: int(value) for key, value in metrics.items() if key.startswith("debug/resident_ck_")
  1402	                    }
  1403	                    if resident_canary:
  1404	                        if previous_resident_ck_out:
  1405	                            resident_canary["debug/resident_ck_mismatch"] = sum(
  1406	                                1
  1407	                                for key, value in resident_canary.items()
  1408	                                if key.startswith("debug/resident_ck_in/")
  1409	                                and previous_resident_ck_out[key.replace("_ck_in/", "_ck_out/")] != value
  1410	                            )
  1411	                        previous_resident_ck_out = {
  1412	                            key: value
  1413	                            for key, value in resident_canary.items()
  1414	                            if key.startswith("debug/resident_ck_out/")
  1415	                        }
  1416	                        levanter.tracker.log(resident_canary, step=step)
  1417	
  1418	                    if watch_stats is not None:
  1419	                        levanter.tracker.log(watch_stats, step=step)
  1420	
  1421	                if checkpointer is not None:
  1422	                    with callbacks.progress_event_scope(
  1423	                        state_callbacks.emit_event,
  1424	                        callbacks.ProgressEvent.CHECKPOINT_STARTED,
  1425	                        callbacks.ProgressEvent.CHECKPOINT_FINISHED,
  1426	                    ):
  1427	                        checkpointer.on_step(tree=state, step=int(state.step))
  1428	
  1429	        except BaseException:
  1430	            logger.exception(
  1431	                "Fatal error in grug training loop; skipping final callbacks/checkpoint to preserve root cause"
  1432	            )
  1433	            raise
  1434	        else:
  1435	            # Mirror classic trainer behavior: force callbacks on the last completed step.

     1	# Campaign: ragged EP hero MFU loop (loop-260828-8753mfu)
     2	
     3	Goal: raise hero MFU beyond the #8753 stack's frontier without fidelity loss
     4	(no added quantization; loss trajectory equivalent or better) and without
     5	breaking hero-checkpoint compatibility (every keep must hot-swap into the live
     6	hero run; checkpoint migration OK, restore from hero checkpoints must work).
     7	
     8	Cost model (user, 2026-08-29): rack capacity is use-it-or-lose-it — failed or
     9	extra arms are cheap (stay courteous to other rack users). The expensive
    10	failure is a WRONG CONCLUSION: being sent in a bad direction, or prematurely
    11	closing a promising one, because a flawed experiment misled the loop. Review
    12	cycles and arm design optimize for inferential validity first, rack frugality
    13	second. Concretely: every treatment arm pre-declares an ENGAGEMENT CHECK
    14	(evidence the lever actually engaged — a kernel name in a profile, an echoed
    15	flag, an HLO property, or a metric that cannot move unless the lever did);
    16	"no effect" results are only recorded as dead after engagement is confirmed;
    17	near-threshold results get more draws rather than a coin-flip call.
    18	
    19	## Base
    20	
    21	- Branch `research/mcwitt/8753-mfu-loop` @ 04026e94fb = PR #8753 head
    22	  (`mcwitt/8317-hero-step-optims`, based on xla-fork/#8684).
    23	- Contents: offload_carry remat + latency-hiding scheduler (forced
    24	  overlap-limit 1), bitcast pair-interleave, FA4 128x64 wide forward tile as
    25	  `gpu_fa4_cute_wide` (landed by implementation name; dispatch has passed unit
    26	  tests but has NOT yet run on a rack — the iteration-0 control validates it).
    27	- PJRT wheel: `jax_cuda13_pjrt-0.11.1+marin.c9526e8c0272` pinned in
    28	  pyproject/uv.lock (GitHub release, marin-xla-pjrt-20260828). No wheel
    29	  overlay in arms.
    30	- Reference numbers: 24k-restore same-night pair (2026-08-28): control (#8753
    31	  stack sans tile) 23.06/23.05, +tile 23.32/23.37 MFU. 30k-restore cross-ref:
    32	  plain-#8684 gate 22.31 median. Protocols are not comparable; this campaign
    33	  re-baselines at 30k.
    34	
    35	## Fixed protocol (every arm)
    36	
    37	- One NVL72 rack, cw-us-east-08a, `experiments.grug.moe_hero_ep.launch_diagnostics`,
    38	  one arm on the rack at a time.
    39	- Restore from the latest hero permanent checkpoint
    40	  `s3://marin-us-east-02a/marin/grug/hero-12d8b6f0-dee637/2026.08.19.2/checkpoints/step-30000`
    41	  (verified complete: manifest.json present). `--num-steps 30030` (ABSOLUTE
    42	  stop step: checkpoint step + 30; `--num-steps 30` exits vacuously right
    43	  after restore).
    44	- Mixture data (trained-router routing), cf 1.15, `ragged_all_to_all`,
    45	  `--master-params device` (this branch renamed the master-less mode
    46	  `disabled` -> `device`; in-process migration reads a master-bearing
    47	  checkpoint's fp32 master directly into device params), schedule-steps
    48	  4470000, no checkpoints/evals. Profiler off on scored arms EXCEPT an
    49	  allowed profile tail: `PROFILE_STEPS` few, `PROFILE_START_STEP` ABSOLUTE
    50	  (e.g. 30021) so it lands past the scoring window — profiler start_step is
    51	  a global step; a relative value like 5 silently never fires on a restore.
    52	- `JAX_COMPILATION_CACHE_DIR` rotated per run id (clique-init deadlock dodge)
    53	  → every arm cold-compiles ~9.5 min; pre-phase (env sync + restore +
    54	  migration) ~19 min cold, less when the object cache is warm.
    55	- Priority: production allowed (user 2026-08-28) with job runtime capped
    56	  under 1 hour: iris `--timeout 3500`. Watchdog: COMPILE_CEILING=1800 from
    57	  first W&B-run existence, STEP_BUDGET=1200 (20-min post-compile cap, user),
    58	  early release at +19 steps, cancel on preemption/failure/coord death;
    59	  QUEUE_CEILING bounds pending time (queueing doesn't count against the 1h).
    60	- SERIALIZATION: one arm on the rack at a time, and arm N+1 is submitted only
    61	  after arm N's watchdog exits — the coordinator's iris `--timeout 3500`
    62	  clock starts when the coordinator starts (it schedules instantly and waits
    63	  on its child), so an arm queued behind another arm burns its 1h budget in
    64	  the queue and gets killed just as its train job starts.
    65	- RESUBMISSION: an invalid arm (watchdog kill, preemption, incomplete
    66	  window) is resubmitted with a FRESH RID and VERSION, never reused — a
    67	  reused RID merges W&B histories (silent window mixing), reuses the
    68	  leader-populated compile cache (the documented clique-init deadlock
    69	  recipe), and can vacuously reuse the artifact layer.
    70	- Hero liveness (CORRECTED 2026-08-29): the hero IS live, training on its
    71	  own ~11 racks of cw-us-east-08a (177 pods, `iris job list` does not show
    72	  it — check pods/Kueue, not job-list greps). This is the normal operating
    73	  condition; the campaign uses the single leftover NVL72 rack and contends
    74	  only with other experiments (mokcamp, zack gangs) via same-band FIFO, no
    75	  preemption. Occupancy check before each arm: Kueue workloads + pods on
    76	  the 08a context, not `iris job list`.
    77	- TIMEOUT SIZING (lesson from the mfl-ctrl-a crash): iris `--timeout` on
    78	  the coordinator covers QUEUE WAIT plus run; a 3500s timeout dies in the
    79	  Kueue gate behind a one-hour occupant. Rack occupancy is intrinsically
    80	  bounded by NUM_STEPS=30030 (~30 steps past restore, <1h even with no
    81	  watchdog), so the <1h-runtime rule is enforced by the watchdog from
    82	  admission (compile ceiling starts at W&B-run existence) while ARM_TIMEOUT
    83	  is sized for the queue: default 28800 (8h) with watchdog
    84	  QUEUE_CEILING=25200 (7h) cancelling a still-gated gang first.
    85	- Rack etiquette: queue arms back-to-back while work is pending; do not
    86	  submit anything if the loop is blocked >1h on analysis.
    87	- Timestamp bookkeeping (calibration): for each arm record W&B-run-created
    88	  -> first-step wall time. The compile ceiling starts at run creation, which
    89	  is BEFORE restore + master migration + compile; a ceiling kill on a cold
    90	  arm is not evidence against the arm until these timings say the ceiling
    91	  actually fits the work.
    92	
    93	## Metric and guards
    94	
    95	- Metric (CALIBRATED at iteration 0, before any treatment comparison): the
    96	  primary decision statistic is the RUN MEDIAN of `throughput/mfu` over
    97	  scored steps +5..+19. Iteration-0 controls showed sparse environmental
    98	  stall steps (cross-node skew stragglers, 0/4/4 per draw, each -1.5 to -3
    99	  MFU for one step) that dominate run means (sd 0.255) while medians stay
   100	  tight (sd 0.053) — same phenomenon and remedy as the 260811 loop's
   101	  median switch. Stall count (steps >3% below the run median) is reported
   102	  per arm and guards the median's blind spot: a treatment whose draws
   103	  consistently raise stall counts is flagged regardless of median. Mean is
   104	  still recorded.
   105	- Guards (from the same run): `moe/drop_fraction` max/mean not worse than the
   106	  paired control; `train/loss` at the window's last step equivalent to
   107	  control (bf16-noise band; 24k arms showed 1.29964-65 across four arms);
   108	  `window_complete` must be true.
   109	- Noise model: only ONE NVL72 rack exists, so there is no placement variance
   110	  (user-supplied fact, 2026-08-29; overrides the ±2pp cross-night figure,
   111	  which came from multi-rack clusters) — residual noise is neighbor jobs,
   112	  clocks/thermals, and run-to-run scheduling jitter. The decision statistic
   113	  is the DIFFERENCE OF RUN-MEDIAN MFU, with run-level sd estimated from
   114	  the >=3 iteration-0 control draws (sd̂_run(median)=0.053; baseline
   115	  medians 23.3208/23.2400/23.3395) and refreshed by later controls;
   116	  per-step points within a run are autocorrelated and are never treated
   117	  as independent replicates. KEEP requires >=2 treatment draws and >=2
   118	  same-session control draws (bracketing), median delta > max(0.15 MFU,
   119	  3 x sd̂_run) = 0.16 at current calibration; a single C/T pair never
   120	  keeps. Calibrated guard bands (2x the worst observed across three C-C
   121	  pairs): drops max <= 1.7e-4; pointwise loss delta max <= 1.05e-4,
   122	  median <= 4e-5. Near-threshold deltas get
   123	  additional draws instead of a call. Periodic re-control draws guard
   124	  against slow drift across the campaign.
   125	- Fidelity gate for keeps: paired-window comparison of the FULL 15-step loss
   126	  series (data is deterministic at a fixed restore step so series pair
   127	  pointwise). The tolerance is CALIBRATED, not assumed: the iteration-0
   128	  control-vs-control pointwise deltas define the null band for this exact
   129	  30k protocol (the 24k figures are a different protocol). Gate on the
   130	  median pointwise delta plus the calibrated max (median-not-max
   131	  discriminates bf16 reduction noise from a real bug — prior campaign
   132	  lesson); drops get a tolerance from the same control pairs rather than a
   133	  raw "not worse" coin flip. A small exceedance triggers another paired
   134	  draw or a numerical investigation, NOT an automatic kill; non-finite
   135	  loss, materially changed drops, or a deviation beyond the control
   136	  envelope is a hard fail. Any keep that changes numerics beyond
   137	  scheduling/layout gets flagged and needs explicit reasoning about why
   138	  the trajectory is unchanged.
   139	
   140	## Iteration accounting
   141	
   142	Per user: only hypotheses that reach the rack count toward the 24-iteration
   143	floor; review-killed ideas do not. Campaign runs 24 rack iterations or 24 h,
   144	whichever comes LAST. Iteration 0 = 30k-protocol baseline (control draw(s)).
   145	
   146	## Hypothesis ledger (priors from prior campaigns; refresh with a new profile)
   147	
   148	Excluded by constraint: fp8 dispatch wire #7665, MXFP8 (quantization);
   149	anything breaking hero restore.
   150	
   151	- H0 baseline+validation: >=3 control draws at 30k to establish the metric
   152	  and size noise. Draw 2 carries a profile tail (PROFILE_STEPS=3,
   153	  PROFILE_START_STEP=30021, past the scoring window) that (a) positively
   154	  confirms the `gpu_fa4_cute_wide` 128x64 kernel engaged on-rack — its
   155	  first rack outing, and cross-night MFU deltas cannot confirm dispatch —
   156	  and (b) refreshes the lever-sourcing profile (H1 folded in: no separate
   157	  profile arm). Draw 2 runs SCORE_MAX_STEP=28: the default early release at
   158	  +19 would cancel at 30019, before the 30021 trace (codex finding). If the
   159	  tail perturbs draw 2's scored window relative to draws 1/3, drop draw 2
   160	  from the noise estimate.
   161	- H1 profile refresh: FOLDED INTO H0 draw 2 (see above); prior profile
   162	  (dk-hero, pre-offload) showed marshal 2.55 s as top lever and a dense-MLP
   163	  leg that turned out to be architecture (2 shared experts + remat) — both
   164	  priors need re-confirmation on the offload/LHS/tile stack.
   165	- H2 marshal-step levers (SO-H2): DEAD 2026-08-29 — the draw-b trace shows
   166	  zero marshal events and 20 ms/step pure starvation; the 2.55 s leg was
   167	  eliminated somewhere in the offload/LHS/tile stack. Do not re-run.
   168	- H3 transport-exposure hiding: the draw-b trace shows 1133 ms/step of
   169	  ragged-a2a EXPOSED (56% of its 2021 ms; 12 chunk ops x 48 launches).
   170	  Transport is SM-bound (prior campaign), so the lever is overlap/
   171	  pipelining of chunk ops with expert GEMMs, not a faster transport.
   172	  Needs a concrete treatment + engagement check before it can run.
   173	- H9 loop-carry D2D copies (NEW, from draw-b trace): 785 ms/step of
   174	  compute-stream MemcpyD2D — nine copy.7xx ops moving 1.85-3.22 GB, 48
   175	  launches each, per-layer while-loop buffer copies. Candidate: buffer
   176	  donation/aliasing in the layer loop, or moving them off the compute
   177	  stream. Code-level XLA/jax lever; needs investigation of which HLO
   178	  buffers these are before a treatment exists.
   179	- H10 offload-copy prefetch (NEW, from draw-b trace): 576 ms/step of
   180	  layer-carry D2H/H2D exposed while compute idles (~4-5 ms x ~90-140
   181	  copies/stream). Candidate: earlier prefetch / later writeback in the
   182	  offload schedule; interacts with H6 (slop-90 may change remat around the
   183	  offload names). Engagement check: exposed-memcpy time in a repeat
   184	  profile tail.
   185	- H4 capacity-factor titration at the restored router: hero drops at cf 1.15
   186	  are ~5e-5 — far under any budget; cf 1.10/1.05 shrinks transport+GEMM
   187	  buffers. GATE (review finding): a cf keep changes training dynamics and
   188	  its drop cost varies with data over the whole run — a 15-step window
   189	  cannot bound it. Pre-declared acceptance: drop_fraction_max <= 1e-3 in
   190	  the window AND loss series within the control band; even then a cf keep
   191	  is flagged for explicit user sign-off before hero promotion, and the
   192	  prior loop note (2026-08-12: CF moves are frontier-walking) is quoted in
   193	  the decision. NOTE (codex): the 15-step window cannot establish whole-run
   194	  loss equivalence for a dynamics-touching change; H4's in-window
   195	  measurement establishes the THROUGHPUT effect only, and any promotion
   196	  decision belongs to the user.
   197	- H5 overlap-limit re-titration: DEAD AS A FLAG ARM on this base —
   198	  `offload_carry` force-sets overlap-limit 1 non-overridably and
   199	  `_apply_hero_ep_runtime_defaults` silently strips conflicting
   200	  ARM_XLA_FLAGS (a flag-based treatment would run as an exact control).
   201	  Testing overlap>1 means changing the remat mode, which is a different
   202	  hypothesis (and the documented silent-corruption recipe with LHS +
   203	  offload — see activation-offload-lhs-overlap-race).
   204	- H6 slop-factor raise (user suggestion 2026-08-29, HIGH priority — cheap,
   205	  code-supported mechanism): `--xla_gpu_memory_limit_slop_factor=85` in
   206	  train.py flag_defaults was calibrated for the pre-offload posture (default
   207	  95 sized the temp arena at 125.7 GiB > the 120.2 GiB the pool can serve;
   208	  85 -> 113.6 GiB), and the comment itself says a smaller arena makes
   209	  HloRematerialization recompute more of the step. The carry offload since
   210	  dropped peak from ~138 to ~116.5 GiB, so the remat pressure the arena cap
   211	  creates may now be pure waste. The flag IS overridable via inherited
   212	  XLA_FLAGS (verified: only overlap-limit and ragged-required flags are
   213	  forced), so treatment = ARM_XLA_FLAGS with slop 90 (arena ~120.2, at the
   214	  documented bound; ladder to 88 on OOM, 92/95 on green+gain). Fidelity:
   215	  remat changes recompute scheduling only. Engagement check: peak-HBM
   216	  metric must RISE vs control (less remat holds more live); an unchanged
   217	  peak means the flag did not engage regardless of the MFU reading. Failure
   218	  mode is a named first-step NCCL allocation abort — cheap crash, ladder
   219	  down.
   220	- H7 attn-offload re-add: 23.14 but 3x stdev and the host allocation lands as
   221	  one contiguous 72.56 GiB block regardless of HOST_MEM_LIMIT_GB — ledger
   222	  says dead unless pod memory grows.
   223	- H8 marin_ep_mgpu_fused transport on this stack: mok-goal showed 22.83 vs
   224	  22.30 control at 18k restore with 16x fewer drops, but needs the kmax128
   225	  wheel (conflicts with the pinned c9526e wheel) and the backend isn't on
   226	  this branch. Big integration; park unless transport dominates H1 profile
   227	  and cheaper levers dry up.
   228	- Dead-lever ledger (do not re-run without new evidence): PGLE (null),
   229	  command buffers at hero shape (+7.5 GiB, negative), FA4 backward tile
   230	  (hard-locked 64x64), 128x128 forward tile (2.5x regression), fused shared
   231	  experts (~0), small-param FSDP (negative), unchaining per-chunk a2a
   232	  buffers (-0.13), NVLS/SHARP env (null), fa4-lse primal output (moot: XLA
   233	  already device-saves FA4 out/lse).

   102	## H6 arm spec (iteration 1 candidate, pending its own review)
   103	
   104	Treatment: `ARM_XLA_FLAGS="--xla_gpu_memory_limit_slop_factor=90"`, everything
   105	else identical to control; env-only (no commit), recorded in arms.tsv extra
   106	column. Mechanism: raises the scheduler/remat temp-arena cap from 113.6 GiB
   107	(85) to ~120.2 GiB (90, at the documented pool-free-space bound); the 85
   108	calibration predates the carry offload (peak now ~116.5 GiB, was ~138) and
   109	train.py's own comment says the smaller arena makes HloRematerialization
   110	recompute more of the step.
   111	
   112	Draws: one T at slop90; if delta > bar candidate, a second T draw; controls
   113	bracket from iteration-0 draws plus a fresh control after (the keep rule's
   114	>=2C/>=2T). Ladder: OOM at 90 -> 88; green+gain at 90 -> consider 92 with the
   115	explicit caveat that arena 122.9 GiB exceeds the documented 120.2 bound and
   116	must survive a fresh-mapping against ~17 GiB physical outside the pool.
   117	
   118	Engagement check: memory/peak_gib (scored as peak_gib_max) must RISE vs
   119	control. MFU moved but peak did not -> not engaged, inspect task-log XLA_FLAGS
   120	echo before concluding anything. Failure modes: named first-step NCCL alloc
   121	abort or BFC OOM (cheap crash, ladder down).
   122	
   123	Confounds pre-declared: (a) remat disengaging when peak crosses the allocator
   124	boundary is the DESIRED mechanism here, not an artifact (#8054's trap inverted)
   125	— but drops and the paired loss series must stay clean; (b) cuda_async
   126	MEM_FRACTION is a RELEASE threshold (#8490): if the higher peak crosses it,
   127	the pool unmaps every step and the arm gets slower with high step-time
   128	variance — watch mfu_stdev, and treat a high-variance slowdown as
   129	threshold-crossing, not as "slop raise is bad".
   130	
   131	## H6 review dispositions (fable + codex, 2026-08-29; transcripts: review-codex-h6.txt)
   132	
   133	Both reviewers verified the flag path end-to-end (arm.sh -> coordinator ->
   134	XLA_ forwarding -> explicit-wins dedupe in _apply_hero_ep_runtime_defaults ->
   135	exactly one slop token =90 reaches the task; last-occurrence-wins in XLA's
   136	parser if ever duplicated). Both BLOCKED the spec as written. Revised spec:
   137	
   138	- ENGAGEMENT REDEFINED (codex blocker 1 / fable F2): slop sets a scheduler
   139	  and remat LIMIT, not an allocation; peak_bytes_in_use can stay flat under
   140	  genuine engagement and can rise for unrelated reasons (rank-0 lifetime
   141	  high-water incl. restore/migration transients). New scheme:
   142	  (1) config engagement — the flag present exactly once in the task env
   143	  (verified by local pre-flight of _apply_hero_ep_runtime_defaults + task
   144	  log); (2) mechanism evidence — compile-time remat/scheduler logs:
   145	  dispatch.py now forwards TF_CPP_MIN_LOG_LEVEL/TF_CPP_VMODULE and every arm
   146	  runs TF_CPP_MIN_LOG_LEVEL=0 TF_CPP_VMODULE=hlo_rematerialization=1, so
   147	  control and treatment logs carry the LHS limit line and remat stats.
   148	  peak_gib demoted to supporting telemetry.
   149	- OUTCOME SPACE PRE-DECLARED (fable F2a): {flag confirmed, remat/LHS logs
   150	  unchanged, peak flat, MFU flat} = DEAD-ON-THIS-STACK (a legitimate
   151	  conclusion, not instrumentation failure). BINDING PRE-CHECK: iteration-0
   152	  control logs are read FIRST — if control shows zero remat and LHS usage
   153	  well under the 113.6 limit, H6 closes as not-binding WITHOUT a treatment
   154	  arm.

   222	    env_defaults = dict(HERO_EP_RUNTIME_ENV)
   223	    if processes_per_task > 1:
   224	        # With one process per GPU, the per-process CUPTI sessions collide with each
   225	        # other and with CoreWeave's DCGM, so PGLE cannot profile and its recompile
   226	        # machinery only adds failure modes. Default it off; an explicit env wins.
   227	        env_defaults["JAX_ENABLE_PGLE"] = "false"
   228	    for name, value in env_defaults.items():
   229	        os.environ.setdefault(name, value)
   230	    xla_flags = os.environ.get("XLA_FLAGS", "").split()
   231	    ragged = moe_implementation == RAGGED_MOE_IMPLEMENTATION
   232	    if ragged:
   233	        overlap_limit = RAGGED_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT
   234	    elif inline_watch_enabled:
   235	        overlap_limit = INLINE_WATCH_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT
   236	    else:
   237	        overlap_limit = DEFAULT_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT
   238	    # The scheduler's longer buffer live ranges fit on the ragged transport only once the layer
   239	    # carry leaves HBM. Without that offload its first-step NCCL allocations fail.
   240	    latency_hiding = not ragged or remat_mode == "offload_carry"
   241	    flag_defaults = (
   242	        f"{XLA_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_FLAG}={overlap_limit}",
   243	        f"--xla_gpu_enable_latency_hiding_scheduler={'true' if latency_hiding else 'false'}",
   244	        # The scheduler sizes the single `jit_train_step` temp arena against this percentage of
   245	        # its memory budget, roughly `133.6 GiB x percentage`. The pool holds 138.2 GiB and
   246	        # persistent state occupies 18.1 GiB of it, so an arena above 120.2 GiB cannot be served
   247	        # from pool free space and forces a fresh mapping against the ~17 GiB of physical memory
   248	        # outside the pool. The default 95 asks for 125.7 GiB and fails that way. 85 sizes the
   249	        # arena at 113.6 GiB, leaving enough slack for per-node variation in fragmentation. A
   250	        # lower percentage costs throughput, because a smaller arena makes `HloRematerialization`
   251	        # recompute more of the step.
   252	        "--xla_gpu_memory_limit_slop_factor=85",
   253	        XLA_DISABLE_GPU_COMMAND_BUFFER_FLAG,
   254	    )
   255	    explicit_names = {flag.partition("=")[0] for flag in xla_flags}
   256	    xla_flags.extend(flag for flag in flag_defaults if flag.partition("=")[0] not in explicit_names)
   257	    if remat_mode == "offload_carry":
   258	        # Not overridable, unlike the defaults above, including the scheduler flag it pairs with.
   259	        # Both flags can be inherited from another recipe, but only this one fails silently: a
   260	        # wrong overlap limit corrupts training, while an inherited scheduler setting either
   261	        # aborts in NCCL's first-step allocation and names itself, or runs the slower posture.
   262	        xla_flags = [f for f in xla_flags if f.partition("=")[0] != XLA_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_FLAG]
   263	        xla_flags.append(f"{XLA_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_FLAG}={OFFLOAD_CARRY_COLLECTIVE_OVERLAP_LIMIT}")
   264	    if ragged:
   265	        # Unlike the defaults above, these are not overridable. Selecting the host-launched
   266	        # one-shot kernel needs both flags cleared together plus a splits-per-peer count this
   267	        # branch no longer carries, so honoring a partial override would run a configuration
   268	        # nothing here measures. Drop any conflicting entry rather than relying on which
   269	        # occurrence XLA's parser keeps.
   270	        xla_flags = [f for f in xla_flags if f.partition("=")[0] not in _RAGGED_REQUIRED_XLA_FLAG_NAMES]
   271	        xla_flags.extend(RAGGED_REQUIRED_XLA_FLAGS)
   272	    os.environ["XLA_FLAGS"] = " ".join(xla_flags)

    62	
    63	def _no_drop_capacity() -> float:
    64	    """Return the capacity factor at which the transport cannot clip an assignment.
    65	
    66	    A receiver's worst case is every assignment on the expert axis arriving at one of its chunks:
    67	    ``EP_SIZE * assignments_per_shard`` rows against a chunk buffer holding
    68	    ``capacity_factor * assignments_per_shard / chunks``. Size the buffer for that and nothing
    69	    drops, so the factor is ``EP_SIZE * chunks``. It must be computed: a factor that ignores the
    70	    chunk count leaves each chunk holding a fraction of what can arrive, and droplessness becomes
    71	    a property of how a seed happens to route rather than of the capacity.
    72	    """
    73	    local_experts = NUM_EXPERTS // EP_SIZE
    74	    chunks = _EXPERT_CHUNKS if local_experts % _EXPERT_CHUNKS == 0 and _EXPERT_CHUNKS > 1 else 1
    75	    return float(EP_SIZE * chunks)
    76	
    77	
    78	NO_DROP_CAPACITY = _no_drop_capacity()
    79	# At or below the mean assignment count per expert, so the skewed router in `_inputs` drives
    80	# real clipping and the surviving set is worth checking.
    81	SKEWED_CAPACITY = 1.0
    82	
    83	# The maximum allowed relative deviation between the ragged and dense kernels.
    84	#
    85	# Gradients are gated on a median rather than the max. Both paths compute the same mathematical
   205	    w13 = jax.random.normal(k_w13, (NUM_EXPERTS, HIDDEN_DIM, 2 * INTERMEDIATE_DIM), dtype=jnp.bfloat16)
   206	    w2 = jax.random.normal(k_w2, (NUM_EXPERTS, INTERMEDIATE_DIM, HIDDEN_DIM), dtype=jnp.bfloat16)
   207	    return x, selected, combine_weights, w13, w2

   210	def _accepted_counts(group_sizes: np.ndarray, capacity_factor: float, assignments_per_shard: int) -> np.ndarray:
   211	    """Rows each (sender, global expert) pair gets to keep, summed over the backend's chunks.
   212	
   213	    Mirrors the backend's per-chunk gate: local experts are split into ``_EXPERT_CHUNKS`` groups
   214	    processed in sequence, each with its own share of the receiver buffer, so an expert competes
   215	    for capacity only with the other experts in its chunk.
   216	    """
   217	    local_experts = NUM_EXPERTS // EP_SIZE
   218	    chunks = _EXPERT_CHUNKS if local_experts % _EXPERT_CHUNKS == 0 and _EXPERT_CHUNKS > 1 else 1
   219	    chunk_experts = local_experts // chunks
   220	    local_capacity = max(local_experts, math.ceil(capacity_factor * assignments_per_shard))
   221	    chunk_capacity = max(chunk_experts, math.ceil(local_capacity / chunks))
   222	    chunk_of_expert = (np.arange(NUM_EXPERTS) % local_experts) // chunk_experts
   223	
   224	    accepted = np.zeros_like(group_sizes)

    70	
    71	class _ExpertMlp(Protocol):
    72	    """Runs the expert MLP over a receiver buffer laid out expert-major.
    73	
    74	    Implementations take both views of the buffer's group sizes: the physical sizes, which charge
    75	    trailing padding to the last expert, and the active sizes, which count only received rows.
    76	    Which one a kernel reads depends on whether it covers the whole buffer or works from segment
    77	    boundaries, so both are always passed and a kernel discards the one it does not use.
    78	    """
    79	
    80	    def __call__(
    81	        self,
    82	        x_dispatch: Float[Array, "C H"],
    83	        moe_w13_local: Float[Array, "Echunk H I2"],
    84	        moe_w2_local: Float[Array, "Echunk I H"],
    85	        physical_group_sizes: Int[Array, "Echunk"],
    86	        active_group_sizes: Int[Array, "Echunk"],
    87	        activation_fn: Callable[[jax.Array], jax.Array],
    88	    ) -> Float[Array, "C H"]: ...
    89	
    90	
    91	def _ragged_dot_expert_mlp(
    92	    x_dispatch: Float[Array, "C H"],
    93	    moe_w13_local: Float[Array, "Echunk H I2"],
    94	    moe_w2_local: Float[Array, "Echunk I H"],
    95	    physical_group_sizes: Int[Array, "Echunk"],
    96	    active_group_sizes: Int[Array, "Echunk"],
    97	    activation_fn: Callable[[jax.Array], jax.Array],
    98	) -> Float[Array, "C H"]:
    99	    """Portable expert MLP over XLA's `ragged_dot`, which covers the whole receiver buffer."""
   100	    del active_group_sizes
   101	    w13_out = ragged_dot(x_dispatch, moe_w13_local, physical_group_sizes)
   102	    moe_dim = moe_w2_local.shape[1]
   103	    gate, up = jnp.split(w13_out, [moe_dim], axis=-1)
   104	    return ragged_dot(activation_fn(gate) * up, moe_w2_local, physical_group_sizes)
   105	
   106	
   107	def _cute_expert_mlp(
   108	    x_dispatch: Float[Array, "C H"],
   109	    moe_w13_local: Float[Array, "Echunk H I2"],
   110	    moe_w2_local: Float[Array, "Echunk I H"],
   111	    physical_group_sizes: Int[Array, "Echunk"],
   112	    active_group_sizes: Int[Array, "Echunk"],
   113	    activation_fn: Callable[[jax.Array], jax.Array],
   114	) -> Float[Array, "C H"]:
   115	    """Expert MLP on QuACK's SM100 grouped GEMMs plus cuDNN grouped weight gradients.
   116	
   117	    The grouped kernels are driven by segment boundaries, so they take the active sizes and
   118	    mask the receiver buffer's trailing padding rather than charging it to the last expert.
   119	    """
   120	    del activation_fn, physical_group_sizes
   121	
   122	    # QuACK, cuDNN Frontend, and CUTLASS DSL are installed only with the CUDA 13 GPU extra.
   123	    from levanter.grug._moe.sonic_cute import _expert_mlp_cudnn  # noqa: PLC0415
   124	
   125	    moe_dim = moe_w2_local.shape[1]
   126	    w13_interleaved = _interleave_gate_up(moe_w13_local, moe_dim)
   127	    cumulative_group_sizes = jnp.concatenate(
   128	        [jnp.zeros((1,), jnp.int32), jnp.cumsum(active_group_sizes).astype(jnp.int32)]
   129	    )
   130	    return _expert_mlp_cudnn(x_dispatch, w13_interleaved, moe_w2_local, active_group_sizes, cumulative_group_sizes)
   131	
   132	
   133	@functools.cache
   134	def _quack_grouped_gemm_available() -> bool:
   135	    if jax.default_backend() != "gpu":
   136	        return False
   137	    if float(jax.devices("gpu")[0].compute_capability) < _SM100_COMPUTE_CAPABILITY:
   138	        return False
   139	    try:
   140	        import levanter.grug._moe.sonic_cute  # noqa: F401,PLC0415
   141	        from levanter.grug._moe.cudnn_wgrad_cute import _cudnn_modules  # noqa: PLC0415
   142	
   143	        _ = _cudnn_modules()  # check if compatible cudnn is available
   144	    except (ImportError, AttributeError) as exc:
   145	        logger.warning(
   146	            "SM100 GPU present but the QuACK/cuDNN grouped-GEMM kernels did not import (%s). "
   147	            "The ragged expert MLP falls back to ragged_dot, which computes the same function "
   148	            "more slowly. Install levanter's `gpu` extra to use them.",
   149	            exc,
   150	        )
   151	        return False
   152	    return True
   153	
   154	
   155	def _select_expert_mlp(activation_fn: Callable[[jax.Array], jax.Array]) -> _ExpertMlp:
   156	    """Pick the fastest expert-MLP kernel this process can actually run.
   157	
   158	    QuACK's kernel fuses SwiGLU, so it only applies to SiLU. Everything else -- another
   159	    activation, a non-SM100 GPU, a TPU or CPU, or a build without the GPU extra -- runs the
   160	    portable `ragged_dot` path, which computes the same function.
   161	    """
   162	    if activation_fn is jax.nn.silu and _quack_grouped_gemm_available():
   163	        return _cute_expert_mlp
   164	    return _ragged_dot_expert_mlp
   165	
   166	
   167	def _unpermute_from_global_expert(
   168	    intermediate: Float[Array, "TK H"],
   169	    sorted_indices: Int[Array, "TK"],
   170	    combine_weights_local: Float[Array, "Tlocal K"],
   171	    *,
   172	    tokens_per_shard: int,
   173	    topk: int,
   174	) -> Float[Array, "Tlocal H"]:
   175	    """Weight each token's expert outputs by its routing weights and sum them."""
   176	    positions = jnp.argsort(sorted_indices)
   177	    if sonic_gather_sum_available():
   178	        # One kernel for the gather and the sum, materializing neither the unpermuted
   179	        # ``[TK, H]`` buffer nor the ``[T, K, H]`` view -- at top-8 that view is eight times
   180	        # the output. It accumulates in fp32 like the einsum below and keeps the routing
   181	        # weight in fp32 through the multiply, where the einsum has to cast it down to avoid
   182	        # promoting the larger operand, so the two agree to a single rounding.
   183	        return sonic_gather_sum(intermediate, positions.reshape(tokens_per_shard, topk), combine_weights_local)
   184	    unsorted = _sort_activations(intermediate, positions)
   185	    reshaped = unsorted.reshape(tokens_per_shard, topk, -1)
   186	    return jnp.einsum(
   187	        "tkd,tk->td", reshaped, combine_weights_local.astype(reshaped.dtype), preferred_element_type=jnp.float32
   188	    )
