   119	
   120	def build_hero_configs(*, num_train_steps: int, batch_size: int) -> tuple[GrugModelConfig, GrugMoeMuonHConfig]:
     1	# Copyright The Levanter Authors
     2	# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
     3	
     4	"""Local Grug MoE backend using Tri Dao's QuACK SM100 kernels (SonicMoE) on B200.
     5	
     6	Dispatch/combine as in ``scatter``, but the expert MLP GEMMs run on QuACK's
     7	``GemmGatedSm100`` / ``GemmDefaultSm100`` via the vendored ``cutlass.jax.cutlass_call``
     8	shim. QuACK does all four activation-path grouped GEMMs (gate/up fwd fused with
     9	SwiGLU, down fwd, and the ``dh``/``dx`` backward matmuls); the SwiGLU backward is
    10	elementwise in JAX; the two weight-gradient GEMMs (``dw13``/``dw2``) stay on XLA
    11	``ragged_dot`` (a different varlen-k grouping). QuACK covers ~2/3 of the MoE FLOPs.
    12	
    13	``_expert_mlp_cudnn`` is the same forward with those two weight gradients moved onto cuDNN
    14	Frontend grouped Wgrad kernels, which is faster than ``ragged_dot`` at the hero shapes.
    15	"""
    16	
    17	import jax
    18	import jax.numpy as jnp
    19	import numpy as np
    20	from haliax.jax_utils import tree_checkpoint_name
    21	from haliax.nn.ragged_dot import ragged_dot
    22	from jaxtyping import Array, Float, Int
    23	
    24	from levanter.grug._moe.common import (
    25	    _CHECKPOINT_DISPATCH_INPUT,
    26	    _CHECKPOINT_DISPATCH_OUTPUT,
    27	    _chunk_capacity_drops,
    28	    _interleave_gate_up,
    29	    _prepare_moe_dispatch,
    30	    _swiglu_gate_up_backward,
    31	    _zero_dropped_assignments,
    32	    _zero_inactive_grouped_rows,
    33	)
    34	from levanter.grug._moe.cudnn_wgrad_cute import cudnn_grouped_wgrad
    35	from levanter.grug._moe.quack_moe_cute import quack_gated_grouped_gemm, quack_grouped_gemm
    36	
    37	# QuACK activation-path GEMM configuration, tuned at the i3072 hero shapes on one GB200.
    38	# Tile (256, 256) beats the (256, 128) default by 1.235x on the gated GEMM and 1.094x on the
    39	# down GEMM. CLC persistence adds a further 1.049x / 1.120x at that tile. Under CLC the two
    40	# GEMMs prefer different clusters: the gated gate/up GEMM stays at (2, 1, 1), while the plain
    41	# grouped GEMMs -- down forward plus the backward dh/dx matmuls -- gain 1.055x at (2, 2, 1).
    42	# All of this is scheduling, so none of it changes the computed function.
    43	#
    44	# These reach `_expert_mlp_cudnn` only. `_expert_mlp` -- the local FSDP path, used by the
    45	# `fsdp-nodrop` and `fsdp-chunk4` ablation arms -- still calls the GEMMs at their defaults, as it
    46	# did before this tuning existed, so nothing regressed. It is untuned rather than deliberately
    47	# tuned differently: the measurements above were taken at the i3072 hero shapes and the FSDP arms
    48	# run d768, so the numbers do not transfer without re-measuring.
    49	# TODO: re-measure these at the FSDP ablation shapes and either extend the tuning to
    50	# `_expert_mlp` or record why the defaults win there.
    51	_QUACK_TILE_MN = (256, 256)
    52	_QUACK_USE_CLC = True
    53	_QUACK_GATED_KW = dict(tile_mn=_QUACK_TILE_MN, cluster_mnk=(2, 1, 1), use_clc_persistence=_QUACK_USE_CLC)
    54	_QUACK_GROUPED_KW = dict(tile_mn=_QUACK_TILE_MN, cluster_mnk=(2, 2, 1), use_clc_persistence=_QUACK_USE_CLC)
    55	
    56	
    57	@jax.custom_vjp
    58	def _expert_mlp(x_dispatch, w13_il, moe_w2, group_sizes, cu):
    59	    """y = down( swiglu( x @ w13_il ) ), grouped by experts. Activation-path GEMMs on QuACK.
    60	
    61	    ``group_sizes``/``cu`` are traced int arrays passed as explicit args (not closed
    62	    over — that leaks under shard_map; not nondiff_argnums — that rejects tracers).
    63	    """
    64	    _gu, h = quack_gated_grouped_gemm(x_dispatch, w13_il, cu, return_preact=True)
    65	    return quack_grouped_gemm(h, moe_w2, cu, b_major="n")
    66	
    67	
    68	def _expert_mlp_fwd(x_dispatch, w13_il, moe_w2, group_sizes, cu):
    69	    gu, h = quack_gated_grouped_gemm(x_dispatch, w13_il, cu, return_preact=True)
    70	    y = quack_grouped_gemm(h, moe_w2, cu, b_major="n")
    71	    return y, (x_dispatch, w13_il, moe_w2, gu, h, group_sizes, cu)
    72	
    73	
    74	def _expert_mlp_bwd(res, dy):
    75	    x_dispatch, w13_il, moe_w2, gu, h, group_sizes, cu = res
    76	    # down backward: dh via QuACK (transposed contraction), dw2 via XLA weight-grad
    77	    dh = quack_grouped_gemm(dy, moe_w2, cu, b_major="k")
    78	    (dw2,) = jax.vjp(lambda w: ragged_dot(h, w, group_sizes), moe_w2)[1](dy)
    79	    d_gu = _swiglu_gate_up_backward(gu, dh)
    80	    # gate/up backward: dx via QuACK, dw13 via XLA weight-grad
    81	    dx = quack_grouped_gemm(d_gu, w13_il, cu, b_major="k")
    82	    (dw13_il,) = jax.vjp(lambda w: ragged_dot(x_dispatch, w, group_sizes), w13_il)[1](d_gu)
    83	    # int-typed routing args get float0 zero cotangents
    84	    gs_ct = np.zeros(group_sizes.shape, dtype=jax.dtypes.float0)
    85	    cu_ct = np.zeros(cu.shape, dtype=jax.dtypes.float0)
    86	    return dx, dw13_il, dw2, gs_ct, cu_ct
    87	
    88	
    89	_expert_mlp.defvjp(_expert_mlp_fwd, _expert_mlp_bwd)
    90	
    91	
    92	@jax.custom_vjp
    93	def _expert_mlp_cudnn(x_dispatch, w13_il, moe_w2, group_sizes, cu):
    94	    """``_expert_mlp`` with the two weight-gradient GEMMs on cuDNN grouped Wgrad kernels.
    95	
    96	    The forward output is masked past the last expert group: the grouped GEMMs write only
    97	    the rows inside ``cu``, and those trailing rows flow on through the unpermute and
    98	    combine, so they have to be zero rather than whatever the buffer held.
    99	    """
   100	    _gu, h = quack_gated_grouped_gemm(x_dispatch, w13_il, cu, return_preact=True, **_QUACK_GATED_KW)
   101	    y = quack_grouped_gemm(h, moe_w2, cu, b_major="n", **_QUACK_GROUPED_KW)
   102	    return _zero_inactive_grouped_rows(y, cu)
   103	
   104	
   105	def _expert_mlp_cudnn_fwd(x_dispatch, w13_il, moe_w2, group_sizes, cu):
   106	    gu, h = quack_gated_grouped_gemm(x_dispatch, w13_il, cu, return_preact=True, **_QUACK_GATED_KW)
   107	    y = quack_grouped_gemm(h, moe_w2, cu, b_major="n", **_QUACK_GROUPED_KW)
   108	    return _zero_inactive_grouped_rows(y, cu), (x_dispatch, w13_il, moe_w2, gu, h, group_sizes, cu)
   109	
   110	
   111	def _expert_mlp_cudnn_bwd(res, dy):
   112	    x_dispatch, w13_il, moe_w2, gu, h, group_sizes, cu = res
   113	    # The cotangent's trailing rows are whatever the caller's buffer held; the grouped GEMMs
   114	    # contract every row they are handed, so they have to be cleared here too.
   115	    dy = _zero_inactive_grouped_rows(dy, cu)
   116	    dh = quack_grouped_gemm(dy, moe_w2, cu, b_major="k", **_QUACK_GROUPED_KW)
   117	    dw2 = cudnn_grouped_wgrad(h, dy, group_sizes)
   118	    d_gu = _swiglu_gate_up_backward(gu, dh)
   119	    dx = quack_grouped_gemm(d_gu, w13_il, cu, b_major="k", **_QUACK_GROUPED_KW)
   120	    dx = _zero_inactive_grouped_rows(dx, cu)
   121	    dw13_il = cudnn_grouped_wgrad(x_dispatch, d_gu, group_sizes)
   122	    gs_ct = np.zeros(group_sizes.shape, dtype=jax.dtypes.float0)
   123	    cu_ct = np.zeros(cu.shape, dtype=jax.dtypes.float0)
   124	    return dx, dw13_il, dw2, gs_ct, cu_ct
   125	
   126	
   127	_expert_mlp_cudnn.defvjp(_expert_mlp_cudnn_fwd, _expert_mlp_cudnn_bwd)
   128	
   129	
   130	def _moe_mlp_local_sonic_cute(
   131	    x: Float[Array, "T H"],
   132	    selected_experts: Int[Array, "T K"],
   133	    combine_weights: Float[Array, "T K"],
   134	    moe_w13: Float[Array, "E H I2"],
   135	    moe_w2: Float[Array, "E I H"],
   136	    *,
   137	    num_experts: int,
   138	) -> tuple[Float[Array, "T H"], Int[Array, ""]]:
   139	    x_dispatch, w_dispatch, token_dispatch, group_sizes = _prepare_moe_dispatch(
   140	        x, selected_experts, combine_weights, num_experts=num_experts
   141	    )
   142	    x_dispatch = tree_checkpoint_name(x_dispatch, _CHECKPOINT_DISPATCH_INPUT)
   143	    moe_dim = moe_w2.shape[1]
   144	    w13_il = _interleave_gate_up(moe_w13, moe_dim)
   145	    cu = jnp.concatenate([jnp.zeros((1,), jnp.int32), jnp.cumsum(group_sizes).astype(jnp.int32)])
   146	
   147	    with jax.named_scope("moe_up_down_quack"):
   148	        out_dispatch = tree_checkpoint_name(
   149	            _expert_mlp(x_dispatch, w13_il, moe_w2, group_sizes, cu), _CHECKPOINT_DISPATCH_OUTPUT
   150	        )
   151	
   152	    with jax.named_scope("scatter"):
   153	        out = jnp.zeros_like(x).at[token_dispatch].add(out_dispatch * w_dispatch[:, None], mode="drop")
   154	    return out, _zero_dropped_assignments()
   155	
     1	# Copyright The Levanter Authors
     2	# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
     3	
     4	"""cuDNN Frontend grouped weight gradients through the CuTeDSL JAX bridge."""
     5	
     6	import functools
     7	import importlib
     8	from typing import Any, NamedTuple
     9	
    10	import jax
    11	import jax.numpy as jnp
    12	
    13	from levanter.cutlass_kernel_cache import cute_launcher_factory, cutlass_call
    14	
    15	# Row count each expert group is padded up to, so every group starts where the kernel's
    16	# tile loads expect it to.
    17	_GROUP_ALIGNMENT = 8
    18	# Vector width the feature (non-grouped) dimensions must divide, which is what the tensor
    19	# specs below declare to the kernel.
    20	_FEATURE_ALIGNMENT = 8
    21	_MMA_TILER_MN = (256, 256)
    22	_CLUSTER_SHAPE_MN = (2, 1)
    23	
    24	
    25	class _CudnnModules(NamedTuple):
    26	    """CuTeDSL and cuDNN Frontend entry points, imported only where the GPU extra exists."""
    27	
    28	    cutlass: Any
    29	    cute: Any
    30	    cutlass_jax: Any
    31	    kernel_type: Any
    32	    weight_mode: Any
    33	    input_order: Any
    34	
    35	
    36	@functools.cache
    37	def _cudnn_modules() -> _CudnnModules:
    38	    kernel_module = importlib.import_module("cudnn.gemm.cutedsl.grouped.wgrad.moe_grouped_gemm_wgrad")
    39	    utility_module = importlib.import_module("cudnn.gemm.cutedsl.grouped.moe_utils")
    40	    return _CudnnModules(
    41	        cutlass=importlib.import_module("cutlass"),
    42	        cute=importlib.import_module("cutlass.cute"),
    43	        cutlass_jax=importlib.import_module("cutlass.jax"),
    44	        kernel_type=kernel_module.MoEGroupedGemmWgradBF16Kernel,
    45	        weight_mode=utility_module.MoEWeightMode,
    46	        input_order=utility_module.WGradInputOrder,
    47	    )
    48	
    49	
    50	@cute_launcher_factory
    51	def _build_launcher(
    52	    modules,
    53	    *,
    54	    expert_count: int,
    55	    max_active_clusters: int,
    56	):
    57	    @modules.cute.jit
    58	    def launcher(stream, mat_a, mat_b, offsets, output, workspace):
    59	        kernel = modules.kernel_type(
    60	            acc_dtype=modules.cutlass.Float32,
    61	            use_2cta_instrs=True,
    62	            mma_tiler_mn=_MMA_TILER_MN,
    63	            cluster_shape_mn=_CLUSTER_SHAPE_MN,
    64	            accumulate_on_output=False,
    65	            expert_cnt=expert_count,
    66	            weight_mode=modules.weight_mode.DENSE,
    67	            input_order=modules.input_order.Tensor2D,
    68	        )
    69	        kernel(mat_a, mat_b, output, offsets, workspace, max_active_clusters, stream, None)
    70	
    71	    return launcher
    72	
    73	
    74	def pad_grouped_rows(values: jax.Array, group_sizes: jax.Array) -> tuple[jax.Array, jax.Array]:
    75	    """Insert zero rows so each contiguous expert group ends on an eight-row boundary."""
    76	    if values.ndim != 2:
    77	        raise ValueError(f"values must be rank 2, got shape={values.shape}")
    78	    if values.shape[0] == 0:
    79	        raise ValueError("values must contain at least one physical row")
    80	    if group_sizes.ndim != 1 or group_sizes.shape[0] == 0:
    81	        raise ValueError(f"group_sizes must be a nonempty vector, got shape={group_sizes.shape}")
    82	
    83	    group_sizes = group_sizes.astype(jnp.int32)
    84	    padded_group_sizes = ((group_sizes + _GROUP_ALIGNMENT - 1) // _GROUP_ALIGNMENT) * _GROUP_ALIGNMENT
    85	    active_offsets = jnp.cumsum(group_sizes, dtype=jnp.int32)
    86	    padded_offsets = jnp.cumsum(padded_group_sizes, dtype=jnp.int32)
    87	    active_starts = jnp.concatenate([jnp.zeros((1,), jnp.int32), active_offsets[:-1]])
    88	    padded_starts = jnp.concatenate([jnp.zeros((1,), jnp.int32), padded_offsets[:-1]])
    89	
    90	    # At most seven rows are inserted per group. Keep a static worst-case tail so the output shape
    91	    # does not depend on the runtime routing counts.
    92	    padded_capacity = values.shape[0] + (_GROUP_ALIGNMENT - 1) * group_sizes.shape[0]
    93	    padded_rows = jnp.arange(padded_capacity, dtype=jnp.int32)
    94	    expert_ids = jnp.sum(padded_rows[:, None] >= padded_offsets[None, :], axis=1, dtype=jnp.int32)
    95	    safe_expert_ids = jnp.minimum(expert_ids, group_sizes.shape[0] - 1)
    96	    rows_within_group = padded_rows - padded_starts[safe_expert_ids]
    97	    source_rows = active_starts[safe_expert_ids] + rows_within_group
    98	    valid = (expert_ids < group_sizes.shape[0]) & (rows_within_group < group_sizes[safe_expert_ids])
    99	    source_rows = jnp.clip(source_rows, 0, values.shape[0] - 1)
   100	    padded = jnp.where(valid[:, None], values[source_rows], jnp.zeros((), dtype=values.dtype))
   101	    return padded, padded_offsets
   102	
   103	
   104	def cudnn_grouped_wgrad(lhs: jax.Array, rhs: jax.Array, group_sizes: jax.Array) -> jax.Array:
   105	    """Compute per-expert ``lhs.T @ rhs`` for contiguous ragged row groups."""
   106	    if lhs.ndim != 2 or rhs.ndim != 2:
   107	        raise ValueError(f"lhs and rhs must be rank 2, got lhs={lhs.shape}, rhs={rhs.shape}")
   108	    if lhs.shape[0] != rhs.shape[0]:
   109	        raise ValueError(f"lhs and rhs row counts must match, got lhs={lhs.shape}, rhs={rhs.shape}")
   110	    if lhs.dtype != jnp.bfloat16 or rhs.dtype != jnp.bfloat16:
   111	        raise ValueError(f"cuDNN grouped Wgrad requires BF16 inputs, got lhs={lhs.dtype}, rhs={rhs.dtype}")
   112	    if lhs.shape[1] % _FEATURE_ALIGNMENT != 0 or rhs.shape[1] % _FEATURE_ALIGNMENT != 0:
   113	        raise ValueError(
   114	            f"cuDNN grouped Wgrad feature dimensions must divide {_FEATURE_ALIGNMENT}, "
   115	            f"got lhs={lhs.shape}, rhs={rhs.shape}"
   116	        )
   117	
   118	    lhs_padded, padded_offsets = pad_grouped_rows(lhs, group_sizes)
   119	    rhs_padded, _ = pad_grouped_rows(rhs, group_sizes)
   120	    modules = _cudnn_modules()
   121	    max_active_clusters = modules.cutlass.utils.HardwareInfo().get_max_active_clusters(
   122	        _CLUSTER_SHAPE_MN[0] * _CLUSTER_SHAPE_MN[1]
   123	    )
   124	    launcher = _build_launcher(
   125	        modules,
   126	        expert_count=group_sizes.shape[0],
   127	        max_active_clusters=max_active_clusters,
   128	    )
   129	    tensor_spec = modules.cutlass_jax.TensorSpec
   130	    call = cutlass_call(
   131	        launcher,
   132	        output_shape_dtype=(
   133	            jax.ShapeDtypeStruct((group_sizes.shape[0], lhs.shape[1], rhs.shape[1]), lhs.dtype),
   134	            jax.ShapeDtypeStruct((1,), jnp.uint8),
   135	        ),
   136	        input_spec=(
   137	            tensor_spec(mode=(1, 0), divisibility=(1, _FEATURE_ALIGNMENT), static=False),
   138	            tensor_spec(divisibility=(1, _FEATURE_ALIGNMENT), static=False),
   139	            tensor_spec(static=False),
   140	        ),
   141	        output_spec=(
   142	            tensor_spec(divisibility=(1, 1, _FEATURE_ALIGNMENT), static=False),
   143	            tensor_spec(static=False),
   144	        ),
   145	        use_static_tensors=False,
   146	    )
   147	    output, _workspace = call(lhs_padded, rhs_padded, padded_offsets)
   148	    return output

    10	# default distributed batch mapping, which includes the cross-slice axis.

    12	Pembed_vocab = P("model", Pbatch[0])

    17	def unshard(x: jax.Array) -> jax.Array:
    18	    return reshard(x, P(None))
    19	
    20	
    21	def _current_mesh() -> Mesh | jax.sharding.AbstractMesh:
    22	    try:
    23	        mesh = get_mesh()
    24	    except ValueError:
    25	        mesh = None
    26	    if mesh is not None and not mesh.empty:
    27	        return mesh
    28	    return get_abstract_mesh()
    29	
    30	
    31	def _mesh_has_axis(mesh: Mesh | jax.sharding.AbstractMesh | None, axis_name: str) -> bool:
    32	    if mesh is None or mesh.empty:
    33	        return False
    34	    return axis_name in mesh.shape
    35	
    36	
    37	def _mesh_axis_size(mesh: Mesh | jax.sharding.AbstractMesh | None, axis_name: str) -> int:
    38	    if mesh is None or mesh.empty:
    39	        return 1
    40	    return int(mesh.shape.get(axis_name, 1))
    41	
    42	
    43	def _batch_axes(mesh: Mesh | jax.sharding.AbstractMesh | None) -> tuple[str, ...]:
    44	    axes = tuple(axis for axis in ("replica_dcn", "data", "expert") if _mesh_has_axis(mesh, axis))
    45	    if axes:
    46	        return axes
    47	    return ("data",)
    48

    50	def _batch_spec(mesh: Mesh | jax.sharding.AbstractMesh | None) -> PartitionSpec:
    51	    return P(_batch_axes(mesh))
    52	
    53	
    54	def _batch_spec_from_x(x: jax.Array, mesh: Mesh | jax.sharding.AbstractMesh | None) -> PartitionSpec:
    55	    sharding = getattr(x, "sharding", None)
    56	    spec = getattr(sharding, "spec", None)
    57	    if spec is not None and len(spec) > 0 and spec[0] is not None:
    58	        return P(spec[0])
    59	    return _batch_spec(mesh)
    60	
    61	
    62	def _is_replicated_spec(spec: PartitionSpec) -> bool:
    63	    return all(axis is None for axis in spec)
    64	
    65	
    66	def _value_spec_or_default(x: jax.Array, default: PartitionSpec, *, replace_replicated: bool = False) -> PartitionSpec:
    67	    sharding = getattr(x, "sharding", None)
    68	    spec = getattr(sharding, "spec", None)
    69	    if spec is not None and not (replace_replicated and _is_replicated_spec(spec)):
    70	        return spec
    71	    return default
    72	
    73	
    74	def _drop_absent_mesh_axes(mesh: Mesh | jax.sharding.AbstractMesh, spec: PartitionSpec) -> PartitionSpec:
    75	    """Replace mesh-absent axes in ``spec`` with ``None`` (replicated).
    76	
    77	    Compact meshes drop size-1 axes (e.g. "expert" when expert_axis_size == 1), so a
    78	    spec that names such an axis would raise. An absent axis has size 1, so replicating
    79	    along it is equivalent to sharding over it.
    80	    """
    81	
    82	    def keep(entry):
    83	        if entry is None:
    84	            return None
    85	        names = entry if isinstance(entry, tuple) else (entry,)
    86	        kept = tuple(name for name in names if name in mesh.shape)
    87	        if not kept:
    88	            return None
    89	        return kept if len(kept) > 1 else kept[0]
    90	
    91	    return P(*(keep(entry) for entry in spec))
    92	
    93	
    94	def _reshard_for_init(x: jax.Array, spec: PartitionSpec) -> jax.Array:
    95	    mesh = _current_mesh()
    96	    if mesh is None or mesh.empty:
    97	        return x
    98	    return reshard(x, NamedSharding(mesh, _drop_absent_mesh_axes(mesh, spec)))
    99	
   100	
   101	def _reshard_for_shard_map(
   102	    x: jax.Array, mesh: Mesh | jax.sharding.AbstractMesh | None, spec: PartitionSpec
   103	) -> jax.Array:
   104	    if mesh is not None and not mesh.empty:
   105	        return reshard(x, NamedSharding(mesh, spec))
   106	    return x
   107	
   108	
   109	_GRUG_MESH_AXIS_NAMES: tuple[str, ...] = ("replica_dcn", "data", "expert", "model")
   110	
   111	
   112	def _compact_grug_mesh_shape(
   113	    *,
   114	    process_count: int,
   115	    local_device_count: int,
   116	    expert_axis_size: int,
   117	    replica_axis_size: int,
   118	    model_axis_size: int,
   119	) -> tuple[int, ...]:
   120	    if process_count <= 0:
   121	        raise ValueError(f"process_count must be positive, got {process_count}")
   122	    if local_device_count <= 0:
   123	        raise ValueError(f"local_device_count must be positive, got {local_device_count}")
   124	    if expert_axis_size <= 0:
   125	        raise ValueError(f"expert_axis_size must be positive, got {expert_axis_size}")
   126	    if replica_axis_size <= 0:
   127	        raise ValueError(f"replica_axis_size must be positive, got {replica_axis_size}")
   128	    if model_axis_size <= 0:
   129	        raise ValueError(f"model_axis_size must be positive, got {model_axis_size}")
   130	
   131	    global_device_count = process_count * local_device_count
   132	    fixed_axes = replica_axis_size * expert_axis_size * model_axis_size
   133	    if global_device_count % fixed_axes != 0:
   134	        raise ValueError(
   135	            f"global_device_count ({global_device_count}) must be divisible by "
   136	            f"replica_axis_size ({replica_axis_size}) * expert_axis_size ({expert_axis_size}) * "
   137	            f"model_axis_size ({model_axis_size})"
   138	        )
   139	
   140	    data_axis_size = global_device_count // fixed_axes
   141	    return (replica_axis_size, data_axis_size, expert_axis_size, model_axis_size)
   142	
   143	
   144	def compact_grug_mesh(
   145	    *,
   146	    expert_axis_size: int = 1,
   147	    replica_axis_size: int | None = None,
   148	    model_axis_size: int = 1,
   149	) -> Mesh:
   150	    """Return the compact explicit mesh used by raw Grug PartitionSpecs.
   151	
   152	    The mesh is always ``(replica_dcn, data, expert, model)``; length-1 axes are
   153	    kept so downstream PartitionSpecs can name "expert" unconditionally. Unlike
   154	    the old local-only layout, ``expert_axis_size`` may span multiple processes,
   155	    e.g. a 32-process job with 4 local devices can build an effective
   156	    ``(4, 2, 16, 1)`` Grug mesh.
   157	    """
   158	    if replica_axis_size is None:
   159	        replica_axis_size = jax.process_count()
   160	
   161	    shape = _compact_grug_mesh_shape(
   162	        process_count=jax.process_count(),
   163	        local_device_count=jax.local_device_count(),
   164	        expert_axis_size=expert_axis_size,
   165	        replica_axis_size=replica_axis_size,
   166	        model_axis_size=model_axis_size,
   167	    )
   168	    devices = np.array(jax.devices(), dtype=object).reshape(shape)
   169	    axis_types = tuple(AxisType.Explicit for _ in _GRUG_MESH_AXIS_NAMES)
   170	    return Mesh(devices, _GRUG_MESH_AXIS_NAMES, axis_types=axis_types)
  1380	                carry_hidden,
  1381	                layer_mask,
  1382	                use_long,
  1383	                use_long,
  1384	            )

  1387	            _scan_layers, hidden, xs=(self.stacked_blocks.stacked, mask_schedule)
  1388	        )
  1389	        # One cross-device reduction for the whole stack of layers, instead of one inside every
  1390	        # scan iteration. See `_local_routing_stats`.
  1391	        reduced_router_stats = _reduce_router_stats(
  1392	            stacked_router_stats,
  1393	            num_experts=cfg.num_experts,
  1394	            num_experts_per_token=cfg.num_experts_per_token,
  1395	            num_tokens=batch_size * seq_len,
  1396	        )
  1397	        router_metrics = {
  1398	            "routing_entropy_per_layer": reduced_router_stats["routing_entropy"],

  1400	            "load_balancing_loss_per_layer": reduced_router_stats["load_balancing_loss"],
  1401	            "router_z_loss_per_layer": reduced_router_stats["router_z_loss"],
  1402	            "qb_beta_per_layer": reduced_router_stats["qb_beta"],
  1403	            "capacity_overflow_per_layer": stacked_router_stats["capacity_overflow"],
  1404	            "sender_capacity_overflow_per_layer": stacked_router_stats["sender_capacity_overflow"],
  1405	            "receiver_capacity_overflow_per_layer": stacked_router_stats["receiver_capacity_overflow"],
  1406	            "margin_min_per_layer": stacked_router_stats["margin_min"],
  1407	            "margin_max_per_layer": stacked_router_stats["margin_max"],
  1408	        }
  1409	        hidden = self.final_gated_norm(self.final_norm(hidden))
  1410	        return hidden, router_metrics

  1413	    def logits(
  1414	        self,
  1415	        token_ids: Int[Array, "B S"],
  1416	        mask: AttentionMask | jax.Array | None = None,
  1417	    ) -> Float[Array, "B S V"]:
  1418	        batch_spec = _batch_spec()
  1419	        hidden, _ = self(token_ids, mask=mask)
  1420	        return jnp.einsum("bsh,hd->bsd", hidden, self.output_proj, out_sharding=batch_spec)

     1	# Copyright The Levanter Authors
     2	# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
     3	
     4	import abc
     5	import dataclasses
     6	import logging
     7	import typing
     8	from typing import Any, List, Optional
     9	
    10	import draccus
    11	import jax
    12	
    13	
    14	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    15	
    16	
    17	class Tracker(abc.ABC):
    18	    """
    19	    A tracker is responsible for logging metrics, hyperparameters, and artifacts.
    20	    Meant to be used with the [levanter.tracker.current_tracker][] context manager, but can also be used directly.
    21	
    22	    The name is borrowed from HF Accelerate.
    23	
    24	    Examples:
    25	        >>> from levanter.tracker import current_tracker, log
    26	        >>> from levanter.tracker.wandb import WandbTracker
    27	        >>> with current_tracker(WandbTracker()):
    28	        ...     log({"foo": 1}, step=0)
    29	    """
    30	
    31	    name: str
    32	
    33	    def _prepare_log(self, metrics: typing.Mapping[str, Any]) -> Any:
    34	        """Convert a ``log`` payload to host data, on the caller thread.
    35	
    36	        Both ``log`` and :class:`~levanter.tracker.background.BackgroundTracker`
    37	        call this. The default pulls ``jax.Array`` leaves to host with
    38	        ``jax.device_get``; subclasses override to also fold in backend-specific
    39	        conversion. Must be idempotent — the background worker re-invokes ``log``
    40	        on the result.
    41	        """
    42	        return jax.device_get(metrics)
    43	
    44	    def _prepare_summary(self, metrics: typing.Mapping[str, Any]) -> Any:
    45	        """Producer-thread counterpart to :meth:`_prepare_log` for ``log_summary``."""
    46	        return jax.device_get(metrics)
    47	
    48	    def _prepare_hyperparameters(self, hparams: typing.Mapping[str, Any]) -> Any:
    49	        """Producer-thread counterpart to :meth:`_prepare_log` for ``log_hyperparameters``."""
    50	        return jax.device_get(hparams)
    51	
    52	    @abc.abstractmethod
    53	    def log_hyperparameters(self, hparams: dict[str, Any]):
    54	        pass
    55	
    56	    @abc.abstractmethod
    57	    def log(self, metrics: typing.Mapping[str, typing.Any], *, step: Optional[int], commit: Optional[bool] = None):
    58	        """
    59	        Log metrics to the tracker. Step is always required.
    60	
    61	        Args:
    62	            metrics: Metrics to log
    63	            step: Step to log at
    64	            commit: Whether to commit the metrics. If None, uses the default for the tracker.
    65	        """
    66	        pass
    67	
    68	    @abc.abstractmethod
    69	    def log_summary(self, metrics: dict[str, Any]):
    70	        pass
    71	
    72	    @abc.abstractmethod
    73	    def log_artifact(self, artifact_path, *, name: Optional[str] = None, type: Optional[str] = None):
    74	        pass
    75	
    76	    def log_html(self, key: str, html_path, *, step: Optional[int], commit: Optional[bool] = None):
    77	        pass
    78	
    79	    def capture_stall_diagnostics(self) -> None:
    80	        """Capture tracker-specific diagnostics before watchdog termination."""
    81	        pass
    82	
    83	    @abc.abstractmethod
    84	    def finish(self):
    85	        """
    86	        Finish the tracker. This is called when the tracker is no longer needed. This can, e.g.,
    87	        force a commit of all metrics.
    88	        """
    89	        pass
    90	
    91	    def __enter__(self):
    92	        # Deferred: tracker_fns eagerly imports the wandb/trackio backends, which import
    93	        # `background`, which imports this module -> a genuine cycle. __enter__ only needs
    94	        # the global-tracker accessor at call time, so import it lazily here.
    95	        import levanter.tracker.tracker_fns as tracker_fns  # noqa: PLC0415
    96	
    97	        if hasattr(self, "_tracker_cm"):
    98	            raise RuntimeError("This tracker is already set as the global tracker")
    99	        setattr(self, "_tracker_cm", tracker_fns.current_tracker(self))
   100	        self._tracker_cm.__enter__()
   101	
   102	    def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc_val, exc_tb):
   103	        if not hasattr(self, "_tracker_cm"):
   104	            raise RuntimeError("This tracker is not set as the global tracker")
   105	        self._tracker_cm.__exit__(exc_type, exc_val, exc_tb)
   106	        delattr(self, "_tracker_cm")
   107	
   108	
   109	class CompositeTracker(Tracker):
   110	    """A tracker that fans calls out to a list of trackers.
   111	
   112	    Exceptions from any single member tracker are caught and logged so that one
   113	    failing backend (e.g. W&B losing connectivity) doesn't take down the others.
   114	    Wrap members in :class:`~levanter.tracker.BackgroundTracker` if you also
   115	    want isolation from latency.
   116	    """
   117	
   118	    def __init__(self, loggers: List[Tracker]):
   119	        self.loggers = loggers
   120	
   121	    def _for_each(self, op: str, *args, **kwargs) -> None:
   122	        for tracker in self.loggers:
   123	            try:
   124	                getattr(tracker, op)(*args, **kwargs)
   125	            except Exception:
   126	                logger.exception(
   127	                    "Tracker '%s' raised during %s; continuing with remaining trackers.",
   128	                    getattr(tracker, "name", type(tracker).__name__),
   129	                    op,
   130	                )
   131	
   132	    def log_hyperparameters(self, hparams: dict[str, Any]):
   133	        self._for_each("log_hyperparameters", hparams)
   134	
   135	    def log(self, metrics: typing.Mapping[str, Any], *, step, commit=None):
   136	        self._for_each("log", metrics, step=step, commit=commit)
   137	
   138	    def log_summary(self, metrics: dict[str, Any]):
   139	        self._for_each("log_summary", metrics)
   140	
   141	    def log_artifact(self, artifact_path, *, name: Optional[str] = None, type: Optional[str] = None):
   142	        self._for_each("log_artifact", artifact_path, name=name, type=type)
   143	
   144	    def log_html(self, key: str, html_path, *, step: Optional[int], commit: Optional[bool] = None):
   145	        self._for_each("log_html", key, html_path, step=step, commit=commit)
   146	
   147	    def capture_stall_diagnostics(self) -> None:
   148	        self._for_each("capture_stall_diagnostics")
   149	
   150	    def finish(self):
   151	        # finish() exceptions are logged and swallowed too; a tracker failing to
   152	        # flush at the very end of a run shouldn't crash the trainer's shutdown.
   153	        self._for_each("finish")
   154	
   155

   161	        raise NotImplementedError
   162	
   163	    @classmethod
   164	    def default_choice_name(cls) -> Optional[str]:
   165	        return "wandb"

   194	class DictTracker(Tracker):
   195	    """
   196	    A tracker that logs to a dictionary. We mostly use this to smuggle things outside of jit
   197	    """
   198	
   199	    def __init__(self):
   200	        self.metrics: dict[str, Any] = {}
   201	
   202	    def log_hyperparameters(self, hparams: dict[str, Any]):
   203	        self.metrics["hparams"] = hparams
   204	
   205	    def log(self, metrics: typing.Mapping[str, Any], *, step: Optional[int], commit: Optional[bool] = None):
   206	        if step is not None:
   207	            self.metrics[f"step_{step}"] = metrics
   208	        else:
   209	            self.metrics.update(metrics)
   210	
   211	    def log_summary(self, metrics: dict[str, Any]):
   212	        self.metrics["summary"] = metrics
   213	
   214	    def log_artifact(self, artifact_path, *, name: Optional[str] = None, type: Optional[str] = None):
   215	        self.metrics["artifact"] = {"path": artifact_path, "name": name, "type": type}
   216	
   217	    def finish(self):
   218	        pass

    46	
    47	def _teardown_wandb_service_bounded(timeout: float) -> None:
    48	    """Tear down the wandb-core service without letting it block the JAX shutdown barrier.
    49	
    50	    wandb starts a wandb-core service subprocess and registers its own atexit hook to
    51	    join it, but every wait on that service is hard-coded unbounded: ``run.finish()``
    52	    -> ``_atexit_cleanup`` uses ``wait_or(timeout=None)`` and the service-teardown hook
    53	    ends in a bare ``subprocess.wait()`` (wandb 0.26.0 exposes no timeout for either).
    54	    When the service wedges on a stuck upload those joins never return, which on a
    55	    multi-slice run holds the primary slice past the JAX distributed shutdown-barrier
    56	    deadline and SIGABRTs the whole job.
    57	
    58	    We run the public ``wandb.teardown()`` on a daemon watchdog thread and wait up to
    59	    ``timeout``. ``teardown()`` unregisters wandb's own atexit hook before it blocks, so
    60	    once it has started the main thread will not get stuck on wandb at interpreter exit
    61	    even if the upload is wedged. On timeout we abandon the watchdog thread — the same
    62	    thing :meth:`BackgroundTracker.finish` already does with its worker — and let the
    63	    process teardown reap the orphaned service. Metrics are mirrored to finelog, so
    64	    dropping the wandb upload tail is acceptable.
    65	    """
    66	    done = threading.Event()
    67	
    68	    def _run() -> None:
    69	        try:
    70	            wandb.teardown()
    71	        except Exception:
    72	            logger.exception("wandb.teardown() raised during bounded service teardown.")
    73	        finally:
    74	            done.set()
    75	
    76	    threading.Thread(target=_run, name="wandb-service-teardown", daemon=True).start()
    77	
    78	    if not done.wait(timeout):
    79	        logger.warning(
    80	            "wandb-core service did not tear down within %.1fs; abandoning it so the JAX shutdown "
    81	            "barrier is not blocked (wandb upload tail dropped; metrics are mirrored to finelog).",
    82	            timeout,
    83	        )
    84	
    85	
    86	class WandbTracker(Tracker):
    87	    name: str = "wandb"
    88	    run: WandbRun
    89	
    90	    def __init__(
    91	        self,
    92	        run: Optional[WandbRun],
    93	        replicate_path: Optional[str] = None,
    94	        suppress_logging: bool = False,
    95	        minimum_log_step: int = 0,
    96	    ):
    97	        if run is None:
    98	            if wandb.run is None:
    99	                logger.warning("Wandb run is not initialized. Initializing a new run.")
   100	                runx = wandb.init()
   101	                if runx is None:
   102	                    raise RuntimeError("Wandb run is not initialized.")
   103	                self.run = runx
   104	            else:
   105	                self.run = wandb.run
   106	        else:
   107	            self.run = run
   108	
   109	        self._last_warning_step = -500
   110	        self._replicate_path = replicate_path
   111	        self._suppress_logging = suppress_logging
   112	        self._minimum_log_step = minimum_log_step
   113	
   114	    # The prepare hooks do the full wandb-side conversion (SummaryStats expansion,
   115	    # wandb.Histogram construction) so the background worker only uploads. They are
   116	    # idempotent, so re-running them on an already-prepared payload is a no-op.
   117	
   118	    def _prepare_log(self, metrics):
   119	        return _convert_metrics_to_wandb_loggable(metrics)
   120	
   121	    def _prepare_summary(self, metrics):
   122	        return _convert_value_to_loggable_rec(metrics)
   123	
   124	    def _prepare_hyperparameters(self, hparams):
   125	        return _convert_value_to_loggable_rec(hparams)
   126	
   127	    def log_hyperparameters(self, hparams: dict[str, Any]):
   128	        if self._suppress_logging:
   129	            return
   130	        self.run.config.update(self._prepare_hyperparameters(hparams), allow_val_change=True)
   131	
   132	    def log(self, metrics: typing.Mapping[str, Any], *, step, commit=None):
   133	        if step is None and not commit:
   134	            step = self.run.step
   135	
   136	        if step < self._minimum_log_step:
   137	            if step - self._last_warning_step > 500:
   138	                logger.warning(
   139	                    f"Step {step} is less than the current step {self._minimum_log_step}. "
   140	                    "Cowardly refusing to log metrics."
   141	                )
   142	                self._last_warning_step = step
   143	            return
   144	
   145	        step = int(step)
   146	
   147	        to_log = self._prepare_log(metrics)
   148	
   149	        if self._suppress_logging:
   150	            return
   151	
   152	        if step < self.run.step:
   153	            if step - self._last_warning_step > 500:
   154	                logger.warning(
   155	                    f"Step {step} is less than the current step {self.run.step}. Cowardly refusing to log metrics."
   156	                )
   157	                self._last_warning_step = step
   158	            return
   159	
   160	        self.run.log(to_log, step=step, commit=commit)
   161	
   162	    def log_summary(self, metrics: typing.Mapping[str, Any]):
   163	        if self._suppress_logging:
   164	            return
   165	        self.run.summary.update(self._prepare_summary(metrics))
   166	
   167	    def log_artifact(self, artifact_path, *, name: Optional[str] = None, type: Optional[str] = None):
   168	        if self._suppress_logging:
   169	            return
   170	        artifact_name = name if name is not None else _default_wandb_artifact_name(artifact_path)
   171	        self.run.log_artifact(
   172	            artifact_path,
   173	            name=_truncate_wandb_artifact_name(artifact_name),
   174	            type=type,
   175	        )
   176	
   177	    def log_html(self, key: str, html_path, *, step: Optional[int], commit: Optional[bool] = None):
   178	        if step is None and not commit:
   179	            step = self.run.step
   180	        if step is not None and step < self.run.step:
   181	            if step - self._last_warning_step > 500:
   182	                logger.warning(
   183	                    f"Step {step} is less than the current step {self.run.step}. Cowardly refusing to log HTML."
   184	                )
   185	                self._last_warning_step = step
   186	            return
   187	
   188	        wandb_step = None if step is None else int(step)
   189	        self.run.log({key: wandb.Html(str(html_path))}, step=wandb_step, commit=commit)
   190	
   191	    def finish(self):
   192	        if self._suppress_logging:
   193	            return
   194	
   195	        # Write the replicate file before touching wandb: run.finish() can wedge
   196	        # past the background finish timeout, and by the time it raises the
   197	        # process is tearing down and fsspec can no longer schedule writes.
   198	        # This write is best-effort insurance — its failure must not keep
   199	        # run.finish() from running — and its summary may miss the final
   200	        # commit, so a successful finish rewrites the file with the flushed
   201	        # values below.
   202	        try:
   203	            self._write_replicate_file()
   204	        except Exception:
   205	            logger.exception("Pre-finish replicate write failed; retrying after wandb finish.")
   206	
   207	        logger.info("Finishing wandb run...")
   208	        self.run.finish()
   209	        self._write_replicate_file()
   210	
   211	    def _write_replicate_file(self):
   212	        if self._replicate_path is None:
   213	            return
   214	
   215	        metrics_file = f"{self._replicate_path}/tracker_metrics.jsonl"
   216	        fs, _, _ = fsspec.get_fs_token_paths(metrics_file)
   217	        fs.makedirs(self._replicate_path, exist_ok=True)
   218	
   219	        record = {
   220	            "config": _convert_value_to_loggable_rec(dict(self.run.config)),
   221	            "summary": _convert_value_to_loggable_rec(_summary_for_replicate(self.run)),
   222	        }
   223	        # Write to a temp name and rename into place so a failed write can never
   224	        # truncate an existing tracker_metrics.jsonl (e.g. the pre-finish copy).
   225	        tmp_file = f"{metrics_file}.tmp"
   226	        with fs.open(tmp_file, "w") as f:
   227	            f.write(json.dumps(record, sort_keys=True, default=str) + "\n")
   228	        fs.mv(tmp_file, metrics_file)
   229	
   230	
   231	def _summary_for_replicate(run: WandbRun) -> dict[str, Any]:
   232	    """Read final W&B summary in a way that survives `run.finish()`."""
   233	    # run.summary can be stale before finish(); _final_summary has the fully flushed values
   234	    final_summary = getattr(run, "_final_summary", None)
   235	    if final_summary is None:
   236	        return dict(run.summary)
   237	
   238	    summary: dict[str, Any] = {}
   239	    for item in final_summary.item:
   240	        path: list[str] = list(item.nested_key)
   241	        if item.key:
   242	            path.append(item.key)
   243	        if not path:
   244	            continue
   245	
   246	        try:
   247	            value = json.loads(item.value_json)
   248	        except (TypeError, json.JSONDecodeError):
   249	            value = item.value_json
   250	
   251	        _set_nested(summary, path, value)
   252	
   253	    return summary
   254	
   255	
   256	def _set_nested(target: dict[str, Any], path: list[str], value: Any) -> None:
   257	    cur = target
   258	    for key in path[:-1]:
   259	        next_val = cur.get(key)
   260	        if not isinstance(next_val, dict):

   247	@TrackerConfig.register_subclass("telemetry")
   248	@dataclasses.dataclass
   249	class TelemetryConfig(TrackerConfig):
   250	    """Configure direct training telemetry."""
   251	
   252	    def init(self, run_id: Optional[str]) -> Tracker:
   253	        process_index = jax.process_index()
   254	        runtime_telemetry.configure(
   255	            _TELEMETRY_SERVICE,

   259	        if process_index == 0:
   260	            try:
   261	                writer = LevanterMetricsWriter.from_iris(run_id, process_index)
   262	            except Exception:
   263	                logger.warning("could not configure direct Levanter metrics", exc_info=True)
   264	                return _NonPublishingTelemetryTracker()

  1015	def _simulate_ragged_a2a(operands, outputs, params):
  1016	    """Reference semantics of ``ragged_all_to_all``: slice i of sender s goes to shard i // spd.
  1017	
  1018	    Checks the receiver's ``recv_sizes`` against what each sender actually writes. The real
  1019	    collective sizes incoming transfers from that vector, so building it from the wrong direction
  1020	    -- the easiest mistake in this arithmetic, since the two are transposes of one another -- moves
  1021	    the right bytes here but mis-sizes the receive on a real multi-shard run.
  1022	    """
  1023	    num_shards = len(operands)
  1024	    for sender in range(num_shards):
  1025	        in_off, send, out_off, _ = (np.asarray(a) for a in params[sender])
  1026	        slices_per_device = len(in_off) // num_shards
  1027	        for i in range(len(in_off)):
  1028	            dst = i // slices_per_device
  1029	            n = send[i]
  1030	            recv = np.asarray(params[dst].recv_sizes)[sender * slices_per_device + i % slices_per_device]
  1031	            assert recv == n, f"recv_sizes {recv} != send_sizes {n} for update {i} from {sender} to {dst}"
  1032	            outputs[dst][out_off[i] : out_off[i] + n] = operands[sender][in_off[i] : in_off[i] + n]
  1033	
  1034	
  1035	def test_expert_granular_a2a_params_roundtrip_with_drops():
  1036	    """Dispatch packs receivers expert-major with sender order inside each expert, and the
  1037	    return direction restores each accepted row to its unclipped sorted position, leaving
  1038	    dropped rows at the output operand's values -- all under forced capacity clipping."""
  1039	    shards, local_experts, tokens, topk, hidden = 4, 3, 10, 2, 5
  1040	    num_experts = shards * local_experts
  1041	    assignments = tokens * topk
  1042	    capacity = int(0.7 * assignments)  # force drops
  1043	
  1044	    rng = np.random.default_rng(0)
  1045	    selected = rng.integers(0, num_experts, size=(shards, tokens, topk))
  1046	    payload = rng.normal(size=(shards, assignments, hidden)).astype(np.float32)
  1047	    sorted_payload = np.stack([payload[s][np.argsort(selected[s].reshape(-1), kind="stable")] for s in range(shards)])
  1048	    group_sizes = np.stack(
  1049	        [np.bincount(selected[s].reshape(-1), minlength=num_experts) for s in range(shards)]
  1050	    ).astype(np.int32)
  1051	    starts = np.cumsum(group_sizes, axis=1) - group_sizes
  1052	
  1053	    clipped = np.asarray(
  1054	        _clip_receiver_group_sizes(
  1055	            jnp.asarray(group_sizes), local_expert_size=local_experts, receiver_capacity=capacity
  1056	        )
  1057	    )
  1058	    assert clipped.sum() < group_sizes.sum()  # drops actually happen
  1059	
  1060	    params = [
  1061	        _expert_granular_a2a_params(
  1062	            jnp.asarray(group_sizes),
  1063	            jnp.asarray(clipped),
  1064	            jnp.asarray(s),
  1065	            local_expert_size=local_experts,
  1066	        )
  1067	        for s in range(shards)
  1068	    ]
  1069	
  1070	    received = [np.zeros((capacity, hidden), np.float32) for _ in range(shards)]
  1071	    _simulate_ragged_a2a(sorted_payload, received, [p[0] for p in params])
  1072	    for receiver in range(shards):
  1073	        rows = [
  1074	            sorted_payload[s][starts[s, g] : starts[s, g] + clipped[s, g]]
  1075	            for e in range(local_experts)
  1076	            for g in [receiver * local_experts + e]
  1077	            for s in range(shards)
  1078	        ]
  1079	        expected = np.concatenate(rows, axis=0)
  1080	        np.testing.assert_array_equal(received[receiver][: len(expected)], expected)
  1081	        np.testing.assert_array_equal(received[receiver][len(expected) :], 0)
  1082	
  1083	    returned = [np.zeros((assignments, hidden), np.float32) for _ in range(shards)]
  1084	    _simulate_ragged_a2a(received, returned, [p[1] for p in params])
  1085	    for s in range(shards):
  1086	        expected = np.zeros_like(sorted_payload[s])
  1087	        for g in range(num_experts):
  1088	            expected[starts[s, g] : starts[s, g] + clipped[s, g]] = sorted_payload[s][
  1089	                starts[s, g] : starts[s, g] + clipped[s, g]
  1090	            ]
  1091	        np.testing.assert_array_equal(returned[s], expected)
  1092	
  1093	
  1094	def test_expert_granular_a2a_params_chunked_masking_composes():
  1095	    """Masking the clip to one expert chunk at a time (full sender starts, chained returns)
  1096	    reproduces the whole layer: each chunk's receiver packs only its experts from offset zero,
  1097	    and the chained returns cover exactly the per-chunk accepted prefixes."""
  1098	    shards, local_experts, tokens, topk, hidden = 4, 3, 10, 2, 5
  1099	    num_experts = shards * local_experts
  1100	    assignments = tokens * topk
  1101	    capacity = int(0.7 * assignments)
  1102	    chunks = 3
  1103	    chunk_capacity = -(-capacity // chunks)
  1104	    chunk_of_expert = (np.arange(num_experts) % local_experts) // (local_experts // chunks)
  1105	
  1106	    rng = np.random.default_rng(0)
  1107	    selected = rng.integers(0, num_experts, size=(shards, tokens, topk))
  1108	    payload = rng.normal(size=(shards, assignments, hidden)).astype(np.float32)
  1109	    sorted_payload = np.stack([payload[s][np.argsort(selected[s].reshape(-1), kind="stable")] for s in range(shards)])
  1110	    group_sizes = np.stack(
  1111	        [np.bincount(selected[s].reshape(-1), minlength=num_experts) for s in range(shards)]
  1112	    ).astype(np.int32)
  1113	    starts = np.cumsum(group_sizes, axis=1) - group_sizes
  1114	
  1115	    returned = [np.zeros((assignments, hidden), np.float32) for _ in range(shards)]
  1116	    accepted = np.zeros((shards, num_experts), np.int32)
  1117	    for chunk in range(chunks):
  1118	        masked = np.where(chunk_of_expert[None, :] == chunk, group_sizes, 0)
  1119	        clipped = np.asarray(
  1120	            _clip_receiver_group_sizes(
  1121	                jnp.asarray(masked), local_expert_size=local_experts, receiver_capacity=chunk_capacity
  1122	            )
  1123	        )
  1124	        accepted += clipped
  1125	        params = [
  1126	            _expert_granular_a2a_params(
  1127	                jnp.asarray(group_sizes),
  1128	                jnp.asarray(clipped),
  1129	                jnp.asarray(s),
  1130	                local_expert_size=local_experts,
  1131	            )
  1132	            for s in range(shards)
  1133	        ]
  1134	        received = [np.zeros((chunk_capacity, hidden), np.float32) for _ in range(shards)]
  1135	        _simulate_ragged_a2a(sorted_payload, received, [p[0] for p in params])
  1136	        _simulate_ragged_a2a(received, returned, [p[1] for p in params])
  1137	
  1138	    for s in range(shards):
  1139	        expected = np.zeros_like(sorted_payload[s])
  1140	        for g in range(num_experts):
  1141	            expected[starts[s, g] : starts[s, g] + accepted[s, g]] = sorted_payload[s][
  1142	                starts[s, g] : starts[s, g] + accepted[s, g]
  1143	            ]
  1144	        np.testing.assert_array_equal(returned[s], expected)
  1145

  1419	def test_ragged_a2a_receiver_clipping_respects_capacity():
  1420	    group_sizes = jnp.array(
  1421	        [
  1422	            [3, 1, 0, 0],
  1423	            [2, 0, 4, 1],
  1424	        ],
  1425	        dtype=jnp.int32,
  1426	    )
  1427	
  1428	    clipped = grug_moe._clip_receiver_group_sizes(
  1429	        group_sizes,
  1430	        local_expert_size=2,
  1431	        receiver_capacity=3,
  1432	    )
  1433	
  1434	    np.testing.assert_array_equal(
  1435	        np.asarray(clipped),
   320	                w2,
   321	                implementation=impl,
   322	                mesh=None,
   323	                report_capacity_overflow=True,
   324	                capacity_factor=capacity_factor,
   325	            )
   326	            return (out * out).sum(), (out, dropped)
   327	
   328	        (_v, (out, dropped)), grads = jax.value_and_grad(loss, argnums=(0, 1, 2), has_aux=True)(x, w13, w2)
   329	        return out, grads, int(dropped.total)
   330	
   331	    def reshard(x, sel, cw, w13, w2):
   332	        return (
   333	            jax.sharding.reshard(x, batch_sharding),
   334	            jax.sharding.reshard(sel, batch_sharding),
   335	            jax.sharding.reshard(cw, batch_sharding),
   336	            jax.sharding.reshard(w13, expert_sharding),
   337	            jax.sharding.reshard(w2, expert_sharding),
   338	        )

   340	    def compare_without_drops(seed: int):
   341	        """Balanced routing at a capacity that cannot clip, so the transport must equal dense."""
   342	        raw = _inputs(jax.random.key(1000 + seed), tokens, skew=False)
   343	        dense, g_dense = _dense_reference(*raw)
   344	        xb, selb, cwb, w13b, w2b = reshard(*raw)
   345	        o_ragged, g_ragged, dropped = loss_and_grad(
   346	            "ragged_all_to_all", xb, selb, cwb, w13b, w2b, capacity_factor=NO_DROP_CAPACITY
   347	        )
   348	        o_ring, g_ring, dropped_ring = loss_and_grad("ring", xb, selb, cwb, w13b, w2b, capacity_factor=NO_DROP_CAPACITY)
   349	        return dense, g_dense, o_ragged, g_ragged, dropped, o_ring, g_ring, dropped_ring
   350	
   351	    def compare_under_drops(seed: int):
   352	        """Skewed routing at a capacity that clips, against dense restricted to surviving rows.
   353	
   354	        The NumPy oracle decides which assignments survive; masking the combine weights with it
   355	        gives a dense reference that computes only those.
   356	        """
   357	        raw_skew = _inputs(jax.random.key(seed), tokens, skew=True)
   358	        xs_raw, sel_raw, cw_raw, w13_raw, w2_raw = raw_skew
   359	        keep = _keep_mask(np.asarray(sel_raw), TOKENS_PER_DEVICE, SKEWED_CAPACITY)
   360	        dense_dropped, g_dense_dropped = _dense_reference(
   361	            xs_raw, sel_raw, jnp.asarray(cw_raw, jnp.float32) * keep, w13_raw, w2_raw
   362	        )
   363	        xs, sels, cws, w13s, w2s = reshard(*raw_skew)
   364	        o_drop, g_drop, dropped_skewed = loss_and_grad(
   365	            "ragged_all_to_all", xs, sels, cws, w13s, w2s, capacity_factor=SKEWED_CAPACITY
   366	        )
   367	        return keep, dense_dropped, g_dense_dropped, o_drop, g_drop, dropped_skewed
   368	
   369	    rows: list[SeedRow] = []
   370	    for seed in SEEDS:
   371	        with jax.set_mesh(mesh):
   372	            dense, g_dense, o_ragged, g_ragged, dropped, o_ring, g_ring, dropped_ring = compare_without_drops(seed)
   373	            keep, dense_dropped, g_dense_dropped, o_drop, g_drop, dropped_skewed = compare_under_drops(seed)
   374	
   375	        grad = _graddiff(g_ragged, g_dense)
   376	        ring_grad = _graddiff(g_ring, g_dense)
   377	        grad_dropped = _graddiff(g_drop, g_dense_dropped)
   378	        rows.append(
   379	            SeedRow(
   380	                seed=seed,
   381	                ragged_vs_dense=_maxdiff_vs_dense(o_ragged, dense),
   382	                ring_vs_dense=_maxdiff_vs_dense(o_ring, dense),
   383	                ring_grad_vs_dense=ring_grad,
   384	                grad_vs_dense=grad,
   385	                dropped_no_drop_case=dropped,
   386	                dropped_no_drop_case_ring=dropped_ring,
   387	                ragged_vs_dense_dropped=_maxdiff_vs_dense(o_drop, dense_dropped),
   388	                grad_vs_dense_dropped=grad_dropped,
   389	                dropped_skewed=dropped_skewed,
   390	                dropped_skewed_expected=round(float((1.0 - keep).sum())),

codex
Verdict: **NO-GO for automatically proceeding from smoke to the scored pair.** The 2-step smoke is reasonable, but scoring should wait for Finding 1. Even afterward, the result is throughput-only—not a fidelity-preserving campaign keep.

- **P1 — Explicit one-chunk execution has no value/gradient oracle.** New tests prove plumbing, rejection, HLO identity for default/`2`, and collective counts, but never prove that explicit `1` produces correct values and gradients. The accelerator correctness harness still invokes `moe_mlp` without `ragged_expert_chunks`, and its oracle hardcodes the default resolver ([ragged_ep.py:320](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/marin/src/marin/testing/moe/ragged_ep.py:320), [ragged_ep.py:340](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/marin/src/marin/testing/moe/ragged_ep.py:340), [test_moe_hero_ep.py:632](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/tests/test_moe_hero_ep.py:632)). Because the hero treatment intentionally changes accepted assignments, a loss difference during smoke cannot distinguish the expected gate change from a one-chunk forward/VJP bug. Required pre-rack evidence: explicit `1` versus dense and explicit `2` at a deliberately dropless small accelerator shape, covering value and all gradients.

- **P1 — The scored result cannot be classified as a campaign keep.** Hero MFU is an analytic constant divided by wall time: neither drop count nor chunk count enters its numerator ([train.py:592](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/train.py:592), [_metrics.py:50](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/callbacks/_metrics.py:50)). Therefore a measured MFU gain cannot arise merely from accounting fewer drops; it must be a wall-time gain. In fact, QuACK uses active group sizes, so fewer drops execute slightly more useful expert work and move more rows ([ep_ragged_all_to_all.py:489](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/grug/_moe/ep_ragged_all_to_all.py:489), [ep_ragged_all_to_all.py:497](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/grug/_moe/ep_ragged_all_to_all.py:497), [sonic_cute.py:64](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/grug/_moe/sonic_cute.py:64)). But the campaign protocol declares materially changed drops/loss a fidelity failure, while `score.py` only reports those fields and does not compare treatment with control ([DESIGN.md:105](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/DESIGN.md:105), [score.py:96](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/score.py:96), [score.py:115](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/score.py:115)). The pair can answer “is this different capacity gate faster?” It cannot establish trajectory equivalence or justify hero promotion.

- **P2 — The +5–9 GiB estimate is credible as a central estimate, not an upper bound.** At the hero shape, changing `C=301,466` to `602,932` adds about 8.63 GiB for one additional half-capacity worth of `x_dispatch`, gate/up preactivation, activated intermediate, and output. The barrier creates a dependency through the previous returned buffer and plausibly serializes chunks ([ep_ragged_all_to_all.py:427](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/grug/_moe/ep_ragged_all_to_all.py:427), [ep_ragged_all_to_all.py:474](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/grug/_moe/ep_ragged_all_to_all.py:474)). StableHLO collective counts do not, however, prove scheduled buffer liveness. The custom VJP also carries `x_dispatch`, `gu`, and `h`, and cuDNN weight gradients construct padded operands ([sonic_cute.py:68](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/grug/_moe/sonic_cute.py:68), [sonic_cute.py:111](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/grug/_moe/sonic_cute.py:111), [cudnn_wgrad_cute.py:118](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/grug/_moe/cudnn_wgrad_cute.py:118)). Those buffers and workspaces could raise the transient delta beyond 9 GiB. The 25.45 GiB stated headroom makes fitting likely, but it is not proven.

- **P2 — Existing telemetry only partially separates fit, pool trimming, and OOM.** `memory/peak_gib` is a process-lifetime high-water mark sampled after the step, and W&B publishes only process 0 ([train.py:635](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/train.py:635), [wandb.py:397](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/tracker/wandb.py:397), [wandb.py:510](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/tracker/wandb.py:510)). Thus:
  - OOM is unambiguous from the failing task log.
  - A successful run with rank-0 peak clearly above `memory/limit_gib` plus repeated high step-time variance is consistent with release-threshold trimming.
  - A peak several GiB below the limit with stable durations supports “fits.”
  - A near-limit two-step smoke is inconclusive: two steps cannot establish recurring variance, and non-primary ranks are invisible. Do not proceed automatically from a gray-band smoke.

- **P2 — Auxiliary tooling remains fixed at the default two chunks.** No code still references the removed `_EXPERT_CHUNKS`; backend call sites resolve correctly. However, `memory_soak.py` and the small-model branch of `restore_benchmark.py` pass the unrelated local/FSDP `expert_chunks` field and never expose `ragged_expert_chunks` ([memory_soak.py:149](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/memory_soak.py:149), [restore_benchmark.py:251](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/restore_benchmark.py:251)). Restore benchmarking is unaffected because chunk count changes no checkpoint shapes; memory soak cannot validate the treatment and will silently exercise default `2`.

- **P3 — “Other transports ignore it” is not entirely true.** Launch validation applies divisibility before checking the selected backend, so an otherwise ignored explicit count can reject a non-ragged run ([launch_diagnostics.py:173](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/launch_diagnostics.py:173), [launch_diagnostics.py:402](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/launch_diagnostics.py:402)). This does not affect the planned ragged arm.

No blocking salt/CTAS interaction found. One chunk removes repeated salted sites; the remaining sites stay distinct, and explicit `2` is byte-identical to the default with the salted machinery present ([ep_ragged_all_to_all.py:226](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/lib/levanter/src/levanter/grug/_moe/ep_ragged_all_to_all.py:226), [test_moe_hero_ep.py:640](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/tests/test_moe_hero_ep.py:640)). The CTAS patch derives launch and barrier registration from the same per-op CTA count, independent of the number of collective instructions; halving launches does not alter its barrier indexing ([h11-dk-cta-cap.patch:14](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/h11-dk-cta-cap.patch:14), [h11-dk-cta-cap.patch:31](/home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop/autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/h11-dk-cta-cap.patch:31)). Verify `cta_count=152` on both arms.

The four focused lowering/plumbing tests passed.
